| [v](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Franciscus_series)[t](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template_talk\:Franciscus_series)[e](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Template\:Franciscus_series) |

**Pope Francis**[^cite_note-3] (born **Jorge Mario Bergoglio**;[^cite_note-4] 17 December 1936 – 21 April 2025) was the head of the [Catholic Church](606848.md) and sovereign of [Vatican City](32408.md) from 13 March 2013 until [his death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_and_funeral_of_Pope_Francis) in 2025. He was the first [Jesuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesuit) pope, the first [Latin American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_American), and the first pope born or raised outside Europe since the 8th-century Syrian pope [Gregory III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Gregory_III).

Born and raised in [Buenos Aires](255919.md), Argentina, to a family of [Italian origin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Argentines), Bergoglio was inspired to join the Jesuits in 1958 after recovering from a severe illness. He was [ordained](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_orders_in_the_Catholic_Church) a [Catholic priest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_priest) in 1969, and from 1973 to 1979 he was the Jesuit [provincial superior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provincial_superior) in Argentina. He became the [archbishop of Buenos Aires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Buenos_Aires) in 1998 and was created a [cardinal](6221.md) in 2001 by [Pope John Paul II](23805.md). Following [the resignation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resignation_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI) of [Pope Benedict XVI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI), the [2013 papal conclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_papal_conclave) elected Bergoglio as pope on 13 March. He chose Francis as his [papal name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_name) in honor of [Francis of Assisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_of_Assisi).

Throughout his papacy, Francis was noted for his humility, emphasis on [God's mercy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/God's_mercy), international visibility, [commitment to interreligious dialogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecumenism_and_interreligious_dialogue_of_Pope_Francis), and concern for the poor, [migrants, and refugees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Refugees_and_migrants). He made women and [laymen](31488305.md) full members of [dicasteries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dicastery) in the [Roman Curia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Curia).[^cite_note-Dallas-2023-5][^cite_note-Faiola-2023-6] He believed the Catholic Church should demonstrate more [inclusivity to LGBTQ people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis_and_LGBTQ_topics), and [permitted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiducia_supplicans) non-[liturgical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_liturgy) [blessings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blessing_in_the_Catholic_Church) of individuals in [same-sex relationships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blessing_of_same-sex_unions_in_Christian_churches)[^cite_note-7] in the [Latin Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Church).[^cite_note-8][^cite_note-9] He also convened the [Synod on Synodality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synod_on_Synodality), which was described as the culmination of his papacy and the most important event in the Catholic Church since the [Second Vatican Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council).[^cite_note-Faiola-2023-6][^cite_note-Horowitz-2023-10][^cite_note-Horowitz-2023a-11] He was well known for having a less formal approach to the papacy than his predecessors by, for instance, choosing to reside in the [Domus Sanctae Marthae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domus_Sanctae_Marthae) guesthouse rather than in the [papal apartments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_apartments) of the [Apostolic Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_Palace) used by previous popes. In addition, due to both his Jesuit and [Ignatian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignatian) aesthetic, he was known for favoring simpler [vestments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vestment) devoid of ornamentation, including refusing the traditional [papal mozzetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_mozzetta) cape upon his election, choosing silver instead of gold for his [piscatory ring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piscatory_ring), and keeping the same iron [pectoral cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pectoral_cross) he had as cardinal.

Concerning [global governance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_governance), Francis was a critic of [trickle-down economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trickle-down_economics), [consumerism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumerism), and [overdevelopment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overdevelopment);[^cite_note-12] he made action on [climate change](5042951.md) a leading focus of his papacy.[^cite_note-13] He viewed [capital punishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_and_capital_punishment) as inadmissible in all cases,[^cite_note-14] and committed the Catholic Church to its worldwide abolition.[^cite_note-15] Francis criticized the rise of [right-wing populism](8354382.md) and [anti-immigration](6491626.md) politics, calling the protection of migrants a "duty of civilization",[^cite_note-Lerer-2025-16] and called for the [decriminalization of homosexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decriminalization_of_homosexuality).[^cite_note-Staff-2023-17] In international diplomacy, he also helped to restore [diplomatic relations between Cuba and the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuban_thaw), negotiated [a deal with China](21396316.md#2018_Holy_See%E2%80%93China_agreement) to define [Communist Party](7175.md) influence in appointing [Chinese bishops](1714754.md), and encouraged peace between Israel and Palestinians, signing the Vatican's first [treaty with Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_Agreement_between_the_Holy_See_and_Palestine) and condemning [Israel's military operations in Gaza](74998519.md), as well as calling for investigations of [war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_war_crimes_in_the_Gaza_war). In 2022, [he apologized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_visit_by_Pope_Francis_to_Canada) for [the Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Canada)'s role in the [Canadian Indian residential school system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Indian_residential_school_system). Following a period of declining health and multiple scares, Francis made his last public appearance on [Easter Sunday](9325.md) before dying on 21 April 2025, [Easter Monday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_Monday).[^cite_note-LastAppearance-18][^cite_note-19] The [2025 conclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_conclave) elected [Leo XIV](54520372.md) as Francis's successor on 8 May, becoming the second pope from the Americas, after Francis.[^cite_note-20]

## Early life



Jorge Mario Bergoglio (fourth boy from the left in the third row from the top) at age 12, Salesian College (c. 1948–1949)

Jorge Mario Bergoglio was born on 17 December 1936[^cite_note-directory-21] in [Flores](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flores,_Buenos_Aires),[^cite_note-Remeseira-22] a [neighbourhood of Buenos Aires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subdivisions_of_Buenos_Aires).[^cite_note-directory-21] He was the eldest[^cite_note-Garrido-23] of the five children of Mario José Bergoglio and Regina María Sívori. Mario Bergoglio was an [Italian immigrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Argentine) and an accountant[^cite_note-biografia-24] from [Piedmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piedmont).[^cite_note-25] Regina Sívori[^cite_note-Sivori-26] was a housewife born in Buenos Aires to a family of [northern Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Italian) origin.[^cite_note-guardian-27][^cite_note-Donovan-28] Mario Bergoglio's family left Italy in 1929 to escape the fascist rule of [Benito Mussolini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benito_Mussolini).[^cite_note-Rosales-2013-29]<sup>: 5 </sup> According to María Elena Bergoglio, the Pope's only living sibling, the family did not emigrate for economic reasons.[^cite_note-fascism-30] His other siblings were Oscar Adrián, Marta Regina, and Alberto Horacio.[^cite_note-31][^cite_note-32] His niece, [Cristina Bergoglio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristina_Bergoglio), is a painter based in [Madrid](41188263.md), Spain.[^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34]

In the sixth grade, Bergoglio attended Wilfrid Barón de los Santos Ángeles, a school of the [Salesians of Don Bosco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salesians_of_Don_Bosco) in [Ramos Mejía](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramos_Mej%C3%ADa), Buenos Aires Province. He then attended the technical secondary school Escuela Técnica Industrial Nº 27 Hipólito Yrigoyen[^cite_note-techniciandegree-35] and graduated with a chemical technician's diploma.[^cite_note-directory-21][^cite_note-36][^cite_note-37] In that capacity, he spent several years working in the food section of Hickethier-Bachmann Laboratory[^cite_note-Clarin-38] where he worked under [Esther Ballestrino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esther_Ballestrino). Earlier, he had been a bouncer and a janitor.[^cite_note-burke-39][^cite_note-Chua-40]

When he was 21 years old, after life-threatening [pneumonia](52135.md) and three [cysts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cysts), Bergoglio had part of a lung excised.[^cite_note-techniciandegree-35][^cite_note-reuters-41]

## Priesthood

### Training and early priesthood (1958–1973)



Bergoglio at an Argentine seminary where he studied for the priesthood in the 1950s

While on his way to celebrate the [Spring Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring_Day_\(Argentina\)), Bergoglio passed by a church to go to [confession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacrament_of_Penance_&_Reconciliation_\(Catholic_Church\)) and was inspired by a priest.[^cite_note-42] He then studied at the archdiocesan [seminary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seminary), [Inmaculada Concepción Seminary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inmaculada_Concepci%C3%B3n_Seminary), in [Villa Devoto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villa_Devoto), Buenos Aires, and, after three years, entered the [Society of Jesus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Society_of_Jesus) as a novice on 11 March 1958.[^cite_note-aicabio-43] Bergoglio said that, as a young seminarian, he had a crush on a girl and briefly doubted his religious career.[^cite_note-celibacy-44] As a Jesuit novice, he studied the humanities in [Santiago](51572.md), Chile.[^cite_note-45]

After his novitiate, Bergoglio officially became a Jesuit on 12 March 1960 when he made the religious profession of the initial, perpetual vows of [poverty, chastity and obedience](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty,_chastity_and_obedience) of a member of the order.[^cite_note-46][^cite_note-Rosales-2013-29] In 1960, Bergoglio obtained a [licentiate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Licentiate_\(degree\)) in philosophy from the [Colegio Máximo de San José](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colegio_M%C3%A1ximo_de_San_Jos%C3%A9). He then taught literature and [psychology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychology) at the Colegio de la Inmaculada Concepción, a high school in [Santa Fe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe,_Argentina), from 1964 to 1965. In 1966, he taught the same courses at [Colegio del Salvador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colegio_del_Salvador_\(Argentina\)) in Buenos Aires.[^cite_note-directory-21][^cite_note-47]

In 1967, Bergoglio began his theological studies at [Facultades de Filosofía y Teología de San Miguel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Facultades_de_Filosof%C3%ADa_y_Teolog%C3%ADa_de_San_Miguel). On 13 December 1969, he was [ordained](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Orders) as a priest by Archbishop [Ramón José Castellano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ram%C3%B3n_Jos%C3%A9_Castellano). He served as the [master of novices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Master_of_novices) for the province there and became a professor of theology.[^cite_note-48]

Bergoglio completed his final stage of spiritual training as a Jesuit, [tertianship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertianship), at [Alcalá de Henares](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcal%C3%A1_de_Henares), Spain, and took final vows as a Jesuit, including the [fourth vow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_vow) of obedience to missioning by the pope, on 22 April 1973.[^cite_note-Rosales-2013-29]

### Subsequent positions (1973–1986)

Bergoglio was named [provincial superior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provincial_superior) of the Society of Jesus in Argentina that July for a six-year term which ended in 1979.[^cite_note-directory-21][^cite_note-sjvows-49] In 1973, he made a [pilgrimage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_pilgrimage) to [Jerusalem](16043.md), but his stay was shortened by the outbreak of the [Yom Kippur War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yom_Kippur_War).[^cite_note-travel-50]

After the completion of his term of office, he was named, in 1980, the [rector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rector_\(academic\)) of the Philosophical and Theological Faculty of San Miguel where he had studied.[^cite_note-51] Before taking up this new appointment, he spent the first three months of 1980 in Ireland to learn English and stayed at the Jesuit Centre at the [Milltown Institute of Theology and Philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milltown_Institute_of_Theology_and_Philosophy), Dublin.[^cite_note-52] He then served at San Miguel for six years until 1986[^cite_note-directory-21] when, at the discretion of Jesuit superior-general [Peter Hans Kolvenbach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Hans_Kolvenbach), he was replaced by someone more in tune with the worldwide trend in the Society of Jesus toward emphasizing social justice rather than his emphasis on popular religiosity and direct pastoral work.[^cite_note-Ivereigh-2015-53]

Bergoglio then spent several months at the [Sankt Georgen Graduate School of Philosophy and Theology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sankt_Georgen_Graduate_School_of_Philosophy_and_Theology) in [Frankfurt](10992.md) and considered possible [dissertation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissertation) topics.[^cite_note-Hochschule-54] He settled on exploring the work of the German-Italian theologian [Romano Guardini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romano_Guardini), particularly his study of "Contrast" published in his 1925 work *_Der Gegensatz_*.[^cite_note-Hochschule-54][^cite_note-litoral-55]

### Return to Argentina (1986–1998)

Ultimately, Bergoglio did not complete a degree there and he returned to Argentina earlier than expected to serve as a [confessor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confessor) and [spiritual director](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spiritual_director) to the Jesuit community in [Córdoba](241269.md).[^cite_note-Hochschule-54][^cite_note-litoral-55] As a student at the Salesian school, Bergoglio was mentored by [Ukrainian Greek Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Greek_Catholic) priest [Stefan Czmil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_Czmil). Bergoglio often rose hours before his classmates to serve [Divine Liturgy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divine_Liturgy) for Czmil.[^cite_note-Vallely-2013-56][^cite_note-greek-57]

Bergoglio was named Auxiliary Bishop of Buenos Aires in 1992 and was [consecrated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_ordination) on 27 June 1992 as [titular bishop of Auca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auca_\(titular_diocese\)),[^cite_note-directory-21][^cite_note-58] with Cardinal [Antonio Quarracino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Quarracino), archbishop of Buenos Aires, serving as principal consecrator.[^cite_note-aica-59] He chose his episcopal motto to be *Miserando atque eligendo*,[^cite_note-60] drawn from [Saint Bede](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Bede)'s homily on Matthew 9\:9–13: "because he saw him through the eyes of mercy and chose him".[^cite_note-Catholic_News_Service-61] In 1992, Jesuit authorities asked Bergoglio not to live in Jesuit residences due to ongoing tensions with leaders and scholars; concerns about his "dissent", views on Catholic orthodoxy, and opposition to [liberation theology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_theology); and his role as auxiliary bishop of Buenos Aires.[^cite_note-ncronline.org-62][^cite_note-63][^cite_note-Vallely-2013-56]

On 3 June 1997, Bergoglio was appointed [coadjutor archbishop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coadjutor_archbishop) of Buenos Aires.[^cite_note-directory-21][^cite_note-jesuit.org-64]

## Archbishop of Buenos Aires (1998–2013)

He became metropolitan archbishop after Quarracino's death on 28 February 1998. As archbishop, he established new parishes, restructured the archdiocese, led pro-life efforts, and formed a commission on divorces.[^cite_note-directory-21][^cite_note-jesuit.org-64] One of Bergoglio's major initiatives as archbishop was to increase the church's presence in the [shantytown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shantytown) (*[villa miseria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villa_miseria)*, or just *villa*) slums of Buenos Aires. Under his leadership, the number of priests assigned to work in the shantytowns doubled, and he visited them himself.[^cite_note-65] This work led to him being referred to as the "*villero* bishop", sometimes translated as the "slum bishop".[^cite_note-66]

Early in his tenure as archbishop, Bergoglio sold the archdiocese's bank shares and moved its accounts to regular international banks. This ended the church's high spending habits, which had nearly led to its bankruptcy, and enforced stricter fiscal discipline.[^cite_note-67] On 6 November 1998, while remaining archbishop of Buenos Aires, Bergoglio was named [Ordinary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordinary_\(church_officer\)#Catholic_usage) for [Eastern Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Rite_Catholic_Churches) in Argentina, who lacked a [prelate](185996.md) of their own church.[^cite_note-aica-59] On Bergoglio's election to the papacy, Major Archbishop [Sviatoslav Shevchuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sviatoslav_Shevchuk) said that Bergoglio understood the liturgy, rites, and spirituality of Shevchuk's [Ukrainian Greek Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Greek_Catholic_Church) and always "took care of our Church in Argentina" as Ordinary.[^cite_note-greek-57]

In 2000, Bergoglio was the only church official to reconcile with [Jerónimo Podestá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jer%C3%B3nimo_Podest%C3%A1), a former bishop who had been suspended as a priest after opposing the [Argentine Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_Revolution) military dictatorship in 1972. He also defended Podestá's wife from Vatican attacks on their marriage.[^cite_note-Magister-2002-68][^cite_note-69][^cite_note-70] That same year, Bergoglio said the Argentine Catholic Church needed "to put on garments of public penance for the sins committed during the years of the dictatorship" in the 1970s, during the [Dirty War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dirty_War).[^cite_note-Magister-2002-68]



Bergoglio in 2008

Bergoglio regularly celebrated the [Holy Thursday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Thursday) [foot-washing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maundy_\(foot_washing\)#Catholic_practice) ritual in jails, hospitals, retirement homes, and slums.[^cite_note-71] Bergoglio continued to be the archbishop of Buenos Aires after his elevation to the cardinalate in 2001. In 2007, shortly after Benedict XVI introduced new rules for pre–Vatican II liturgical forms, Bergoglio established a weekly Mass in this [extraordinary form](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tridentine_Mass) of the [Roman Rite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Rite).[^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73]

On 8 November 2005, Bergoglio was elected president of the [Argentine Episcopal Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_Episcopal_Conference) for a three-year term (2005–2008),[^cite_note-74] and re-elected on 11 November 2008.[^cite_note-75] He remained a member of that commission's permanent governing body, the president of its committee for the [Pontifical Catholic University of Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontifical_Catholic_University_of_Argentina), and a member of its liturgy committee for the care of shrines.[^cite_note-aica-59] While head of the Argentine Catholic bishops' conference, Bergoglio issued a collective apology for his church's failure to protect people from the junta during the Dirty War.[^cite_note-76] When he turned 75 in December 2011, Bergoglio submitted his resignation as archbishop of Buenos Aires to Pope Benedict XVI as required by [canon law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canon_law_\(Catholic_Church\)).[^cite_note-travel-50] As he had no coadjutor archbishop, he stayed in office, waiting for the Vatican to appoint a replacement.[^cite_note-77]

As a bishop, he was no longer subject to his Jesuit superior.[^cite_note-78] From then on, he no longer visited Jesuit houses and was in "virtual estrangement from the Jesuits" until after his election as pope.[^cite_note-Ivereigh-2015-53][^cite_note-ncronline.org-62]

### Appointment as cardinal



Bergoglio on 18 June 2008 giving a [catechesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catechesis)

On 21 February 2001, [Pope John Paul II](23805.md) made Archbishop Bergoglio a [cardinal](6221.md), assigning him the [title](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titular_church) of [cardinal priest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_priest) of [San Roberto Bellarmino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Roberto_Bellarmino). Bergoglio was installed there on 14 October. During his trip to Rome for the ceremony, he and his sister María Elena visited their father's hometown in northern Italy.[^cite_note-fascism-30] As cardinal, Bergoglio was appointed to five administrative positions in the [Roman Curia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Curia). He was a member of the [Congregation for Divine Worship and the Discipline of the Sacraments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregation_for_Divine_Worship_and_the_Discipline_of_the_Sacraments); the [Congregation for the Clergy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Clergy); the [Congregation for Institutes of Consecrated Life and Societies of Apostolic Life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregation_for_Institutes_of_Consecrated_Life_and_Societies_of_Apostolic_Life); the [Pontifical Council for the Family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontifical_Council_for_the_Family); and the [Commission for Latin America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontifical_Commission_for_Latin_America). Later that year, when Cardinal [Edward Egan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Egan) returned to New York following the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md), Bergoglio replaced him as relator (recording secretary) in the [Synod of Bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synod_of_Bishops_\(Catholic\)),[^cite_note-79] and, according to the *[Catholic Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Herald)*, created "a favourable impression as a man open to communion and dialogue".[^cite_note-thunder-80][^cite_note-81]

Cardinal Bergoglio was known for his personal humility, doctrinal conservatism, and commitment to [social justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_justice).[^cite_note-Allen-82] His [simple lifestyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simple_living)—which included living in a small apartment rather than the elegant bishop's residence, using public transportation, and cooking his own meals—enhanced his reputation for humility.[^cite_note-83] He limited his time in Rome to "lightning visits".[^cite_note-falasca-84]

After Pope John Paul II died on 2 April 2005, Bergoglio attended [his funeral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funeral_of_Pope_John_Paul_II) and was considered one of the *[papabile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papabile)* for succession to the papacy.[^cite_note-Los_Angeles_Times-2005-85] He participated as a [cardinal elector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_electors_for_the_2005_papal_conclave) in the [2005 papal conclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_papal_conclave) that elected Pope Benedict XVI. In the *[National Catholic Reporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Catholic_Reporter)*, [John L. Allen Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_L._Allen_Jr.) reported that Bergoglio was a frontrunner in the 2005 conclave.[^cite_note-Allen-82][^cite_note-86] In September 2005, the Italian magazine *[Limes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limes_\(Italian_magazine\))* published claims that Bergoglio had been the runner-up and main challenger to Cardinal Ratzinger at that conclave and that he had received 40 votes in the third ballot but fell back to 26 at the fourth and decisive ballot.[^cite_note-CNN05-87][^cite_note-votes2005-88] The claims were based on a diary purportedly belonging to an anonymous cardinal who had been present at the conclave.[^cite_note-CNN05-87][^cite_note-Rubin13-89] According to the Italian journalist [Andrea Tornielli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Tornielli), this number of votes had no precedent for a Latin American *papabile*.[^cite_note-Rubin13-89] *[La Stampa](1810129.md)* reported that Bergoglio was in close contention with Ratzinger during the election until he made an emotional plea that the cardinals should not vote for him.[^cite_note-tears-90] According to Tornielli, Bergoglio made this request to prevent the conclave from delaying too much in the election of a pope.[^cite_note-91]

As a cardinal, Bergoglio was associated with [Communion and Liberation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communion_and_Liberation), a Catholic evangelical lay movement of the type known as [associations of the faithful](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Associations_of_the_faithful).[^cite_note-Allen-82] He sometimes made appearances at the annual gathering known as the [Rimini Meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meeting_for_friendship_among_peoples) held during the late summer months in Italy.[^cite_note-Allen-82] In 2005, Cardinal Bergoglio authorized the request for [beatification](233724.md)—the third of four steps toward [sainthood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint)—for six members of the [Pallottine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallottine) community murdered in the [San Patricio Church massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Patricio_Church_massacre).[^cite_note-92][^cite_note-CT2005-93] Bergoglio also ordered an investigation into the murders;[^cite_note-CT2005-93] 1984 testimony indicated that they were perpetrated by members of the [Argentine Navy](490409.md) on the orders of Rear Admiral Rubén Chamorro.[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-Andersen-95]

## Argentine government relations

### Dirty War

See also: [Dirty War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dirty_War)

Bergoglio was the subject of allegations regarding the [Argentine Navy](490409.md)'s kidnapping of two Jesuit priests, Orlando Yorio and [Franz Jalics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franz_Jalics), in 1976, during Argentina's [Dirty War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dirty_War). After being tortured in captivity, the priests were found alive months later outside Buenos Aires, drugged and partially unclothed.[^cite_note-Murphy-2013-96][^cite_note-Los_Angeles_Times-2005-85] Bergoglio is widely reported to have failed to protect the priests, and to have dismissed them from the Society of Jesus days prior to their arrest.[^cite_note-nytimes1-97] In 2005, [Myriam Bregman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myriam_Bregman), a human rights lawyer, filed a criminal complaint against Bergoglio, as superior in the Society of Jesus of Argentina, accusing him of actual involvement in the kidnapping.[^cite_note-Los_Angeles_Times-2005-85] While the complaint was eventually dismissed, the debate over Bergoglio's actions during the period has continued, with Argentine journalists relying on documents and statements from both priests and laypeople in reporting that contradict Cardinal Bergoglio's account.[^cite_note-nytimes1-97]

Yorio accused Bergoglio of effectively handing them over to the death squads by declining to tell the authorities that he endorsed their work. Yorio said in a 1999 interview that he believed that Bergoglio did nothing "to free us, in fact just the opposite".[^cite_note-WAPO-98] Two days after the election of Francis, Jalics issued a statement confirming the kidnapping and attributing the cause to a former lay colleague who became a guerrilla, was captured, then named Yorio and Jalics when interrogated.[^cite_note-99] The following week, Jalics issued a second, clarifying statement: "It is wrong to assert that our capture took place at the initiative of Father Bergoglio ... Orlando Yorio and I were not denounced by Father Bergoglio."[^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101]

Bergoglio told his authorized biographer, [Sergio Rubin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Rubin), that he worked behind the scenes for the priests' release; Bergoglio's intercession with dictator [Jorge Rafael Videla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Rafael_Videla) on their behalf may have saved their lives.[^cite_note-Murphy-2013-96] Bergoglio also told Rubin that he had often sheltered people from the dictatorship on church property, and once gave his own identity papers to a man who looked like him so that he could flee Argentina.[^cite_note-Murphy-2013-96] The interview with Rubin, reflected in the biography *[El jesuita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_jesuita)*, was the only time Bergoglio had spoken to the press about those events.[^cite_note-102] [Alicia Oliveira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alicia_Oliveira), a former Argentine judge, also reported that Bergoglio helped people flee Argentina during the rule of the junta.[^cite_note-103] Since Francis became pope, Gonzalo Mosca[^cite_note-104] and José Caravias[^cite_note-105] have related accounts to journalists of how Bergoglio helped them flee the dictatorship.

Oliveira described Bergoglio as "anguished" and "very critical of the dictatorship" during the Dirty War.[^cite_note-106] Oliveira met with him at the time and urged Bergoglio to speak out—he told her that "he couldn't. That it wasn't an easy thing to do."[^cite_note-WAPO-98] Artist and human rights activist [Adolfo Pérez Esquivel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolfo_P%C3%A9rez_Esquivel), the 1980 [Nobel Peace Prize](26230922.md) laureate, said: "Perhaps he didn't have the courage of other priests, but he never collaborated with the dictatorship... Bergoglio was no accomplice of the dictatorship."[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108] [Graciela Fernández Meijide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graciela_Fern%C3%A1ndez_Meijide), a member of the [Permanent Assembly for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_Assembly_for_Human_Rights), also said that there was no proof linking Bergoglio with the dictatorship. She told the *[Clarín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clar%C3%ADn_\(Argentine_newspaper\))* newspaper:

> There is no information and Justice couldn't prove it. I was in the [APDH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_Assembly_for_Human_Rights) during all the dictatorship years and I received hundreds of testimonies. Bergoglio was never mentioned. It was the same in the [CONADEP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Commission_on_the_Disappearance_of_Persons). Nobody mentioned him as instigator or as anything.[^cite_note-109]

[Ricardo Lorenzetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ricardo_Lorenzetti), the president of the Argentine Supreme Court, said that Bergoglio was "completely innocent" of the accusations.[^cite_note-110] Historian [Uki Goñi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uki_Go%C3%B1i) pointed that, during early 1976, the military junta still had a good image among society, and that the [scale of the political repression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detention_Centers_in_the_Dirty_War) was not known until much later; Bergoglio would have had little reason to suspect that the detention of Yorio and Jalics could end in their deaths.[^cite_note-111]

### Fernando de la Rúa

[Fernando de la Rúa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_de_la_R%C3%BAa) replaced [Carlos Menem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Menem) as president of Argentina in 1999. As an archbishop, Bergoglio celebrated the annual Mass at the [Buenos Aires Metropolitan Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buenos_Aires_Metropolitan_Cathedral) on the *[First National Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_National_Government)* holiday, 25 May.[^cite_note-112] During Argentina's [economic depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998%E2%80%932002_Argentine_great_depression), the Catholic Church criticized the government's austerity measures, which worsened poverty. De la Rúa asked the church to facilitate dialogue between economic and political leaders to address the crisis. Although he claimed to have spoken with Bergoglio, Bergoglio reportedly said the meeting was canceled due to a misunderstanding. Bishop Jorge Casaretto had doubted this, noting that De la Rúa made the request only in newspaper interviews, not formally to the church.[^cite_note-113]

In the [2001 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_legislative_election,_2001), the [Justicialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justicialist_Party) won a majority in Congress and appointed [Ramón Puerta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ram%C3%B3n_Puerta) as Senate president. Bergoglio met with Puerta and was positively impressed. Puerta assured him that the Justicialist Party was not planning to oust De la Rúa and promised to support the president in advancing necessary legislation.[^cite_note-114]

During police repression of the [riots of December 2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/December_2001_riots_in_Argentina), Bergoglio contacted the Ministry of the Interior and asked that the police distinguish rioters and vandals from peaceful protesters.[^cite_note-115]

### Néstor and Cristina Kirchner



Francis with Argentine president [Cristina Fernández de Kirchner](1644780.md), holding traditional Argentine [mate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mate_\(beverage\)) drinkware

When Bergoglio celebrated Mass at the cathedral for the 2004 First National Government holiday, President [Néstor Kirchner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%A9stor_Kirchner) attended and heard Bergoglio request more political dialogue, the rejection of intolerance, and the criticism of exhibitionism and strident announcements.[^cite_note-116] Kirchner celebrated the national day elsewhere the following year and the Mass in the cathedral was suspended.[^cite_note-117] In 2006, Bergoglio helped fellow Jesuit [Joaquín Piña](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joaqu%C3%ADn_Pi%C3%B1a) win the elections in the [Misiones Province](233567.md) and prevent an amendment to the local constitution that would allow indefinite re-elections. Kirchner intended to use that project to start similar amendments at other provinces and eventually implement it in the national constitution.[^cite_note-118] Kirchner considered Bergoglio as a political rival until he died in 2010.[^cite_note-anios-119] Bergoglio's relations with Kirchner's widow and successor, [Cristina Fernández de Kirchner](1644780.md), were similarly tense. In 2008, Bergoglio called for national reconciliation during [disturbances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Argentine_government_conflict_with_the_agricultural_sector) in the country's agricultural regions, which the government interpreted as a support for anti-government demonstrators.[^cite_note-anios-119] The campaign to enact [same-sex marriage legislation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Argentina) was a particularly tense period in their relations.[^cite_note-anios-119]

When Bergoglio was elected pope, initial reactions were mixed. Most of the Argentine society cheered it, but the pro-government newspaper *[Página 12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P%C3%A1gina_12)* published renewed allegations about the Dirty War, and the president of the [National Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Library_of_the_Argentine_Republic) described a global conspiracy theory. The president took more than an hour before congratulating the new pope and did so only in a passing reference within a routine speech. Due to the Pope's popularity in Argentina, Cristina Kirchner made what the political analyst Claudio Fantini called a "[Copernican shift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copernican_Revolution_\(metaphor\))" in her relations with him and fully embraced the Francis phenomenon.[^cite_note-120] On the day before his inauguration as pope, Bergoglio, now Francis, had a private meeting with Kirchner where they exchanged gifts and lunched together. This was the new pope's first meeting with a head of state, and there was speculation that the two were mending their relations.[^cite_note-121][^cite_note-122] *Página 12* then [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-censorship) their controversial articles about Bergoglio from their web page as a result of this change.[^cite_note-123]

### Javier Milei

Before [Javier Milei](56599840.md)'s election to the Argentine presidency in 2023, he was very critical of Francis, describing him as "imbecile" and a "communist turd". His disparaging comments sparked controversy among Catholics.[^cite_note-124] However, following his inauguration, Milei softened his position and formally invited Francis to Argentina. Milei visited the Vatican on 11 February 2024, the day Francis canonized [María Antonia de Paz y Figueroa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mar%C3%ADa_Antonia_de_Paz_y_Figueroa), the first female Argentine saint.[^cite_note-125]

## Papacy (2013–2025)

See also: [Theology of Pope Francis § Vatican II revisited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Vatican_II_revisited), and [Theology of Pope Francis § Church leadership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Church_leadership)

[Coat of arms of Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Pope_Francis)



As cardinal



As pope

The gold star represents the [Virgin Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virgin_Mary), the grape-like plant—the [spikenard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spikenard)—is associated with [Saint Joseph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Joseph), and the [IHS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Name_of_Jesus) is the symbol of the Jesuits.

[^cite_note-126]

[^cite_note-OR-127]

[^cite_note-NCR-128]

Francis was the first Jesuit pope. This was a significant appointment because of the sometimes tense relations between the Society of Jesus and the [Holy See](13393.md).[^cite_note-129] He was also the first from [Latin America](18524.md),[^cite_note-130] and the first from the [Southern Hemisphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Hemisphere).[^cite_note-131] Many media reported him as being the first non-European pope, but he was the 11th; the previous was [Gregory III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Gregory_III) from Syria who died in 741. Although Francis was not born in Europe, he was [ethnically European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_Europe); his father and maternal grandparents were from northern Italy.[^cite_note-132]

As pope, Francis's manner was less formal than that of his immediate predecessors, a style that news coverage referred to as "no frills", noting that it was "his common touch and accessibility that is proving the greatest inspiration".[^cite_note-nofrills-133] On the night of his election, he took a bus back to his hotel with the cardinals rather than being driven in the papal car.[^cite_note-134] The next day, he visited Cardinal [Jorge María Mejía](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Mar%C3%ADa_Mej%C3%ADa) in the hospital and chatted with patients and staff.[^cite_note-135]

In addition to his [native](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_language) Spanish, he spoke fluent Italian (the official language of Vatican City and the "everyday language" of the Holy See) and German. He was also conversant in Latin (the [official language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_language) of the Holy See),[^cite_note-136] French,[^cite_note-137] Portuguese,[^cite_note-138] and English;[^cite_note-139][^cite_note-languages-140] he also understood [Piedmontese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piedmontese_language) and some [Genoese Ligurian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genoese_dialect).[^cite_note-141]

Francis chose not to live in the official [papal residence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_apartments) in the [Apostolic Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_Palace) but instead remained in the [Vatican guest house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domus_Sanctae_Marthae) in a suite in which he received visitors and held meetings. He was the first pope since [Pope Pius X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Pius_X) to live outside the papal apartments.[^cite_note-Vatican_guesthouse-142] Francis appeared at the window of the Apostolic Palace for the Sunday [Angelus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelus).[^cite_note-guesthouse-143]

As a Jesuit pope, Francis made clear that a fundamental task of the faithful is not so much to follow rules but to discern what God is calling them to do. He altered the culture of the clergy, steering away from what he named "clericalism" (which dwells on priestly status and authority) and toward an ethic of service (Francis said the church's shepherds must have the "smell of the sheep", always staying close to the People of God).[^cite_note-144]

### Election

Main articles: [2013 papal conclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_papal_conclave) and [Papal inauguration of Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_inauguration_of_Pope_Francis)



Francis appears in public for the first time as pope, at St. Peter's Basilica balcony, 13 March 2013

Bergoglio was elected pope on 13 March 2013,[^cite_note-directory-21][^cite_note-145][^cite_note-146] the second day of the [2013 papal conclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_papal_conclave), after which he took the [papal name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_name) Francis.[^cite_note-directory-21][^cite_note-cnbc-147] Francis was elected on the fifth ballot.[^cite_note-148] The *[Habemus papam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habemus_papam)* announcement was delivered by the [cardinal protodeacon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_protodeacon), [Jean-Louis Tauran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Louis_Tauran).[^cite_note-SuarezPBS-149] Cardinal [Christoph Schönborn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christoph_Sch%C3%B6nborn) later said that Bergoglio was elected following two supernatural signs, one in the conclave—and hence confidential—and one from a Latin-American couple, friends of Schönborn at Vatican City, who whispered Bergoglio's name in the elector's ear; Schönborn commented "if these people say Bergoglio, that's an indication of the Holy Spirit".[^cite_note-150]

Instead of accepting his cardinals' congratulations while seated on the [papal throne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_throne), Francis received them standing, reportedly an immediate sign of a changing approach to formalities at the Vatican.[^cite_note-NewEuropeHabemus-151] During his first appearance as pontiff on the balcony of [Saint Peter's Basilica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Peter's_Basilica), he wore a white [cassock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cassock), instead of the red, ermine-trimmed [mozzetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mozzetta)[^cite_note-NewEuropeHabemus-151][^cite_note-Uebbing-152] used by previous popes.[^cite_note-153] He also wore the same iron [pectoral cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pectoral_cross) that he had worn as archbishop of Buenos Aires, rather than the gold one worn by his predecessors.[^cite_note-Uebbing-152][^cite_note-154]

After being elected and choosing his name, his first act was bestowing the *[Urbi et Orbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbi_et_Orbi)* blessing on thousands of pilgrims gathered in [St. Peter's Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Peter's_Square). Before blessing the crowd, he delivered a brief speech, greeting those in St. Peter's Square with a simple "Buonasera" ("Good evening", in Italian). He thanked the crowd for welcoming him and asked them to pray for his predecessor, "the bishop emeritus of Rome" Pope Benedict XVI, and for himself as the new "bishop of Rome". He also referred to himself as a Pope coming almost from the end of the world.[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156]

Francis held his [papal inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_inauguration) on 19 March 2013 in St. Peter's Square.[^cite_note-directory-21] He celebrated Mass in the presence of political and religious leaders from around the world.[^cite_note-157] In his homily, Francis focused on the [Solemnity of Saint Joseph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solemnity_of_Saint_Joseph), the liturgical day on which the Mass was celebrated.[^cite_note-158]

The next day, [Federico Lombardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federico_Lombardi) told the media that Francis had met all the cardinals in the [Sistine Chapel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sistine_Chapel) standing up, rather than sitting on the chair at his disposal, and that he went back to the *[Domus Sanctae Marthae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domus_Sanctae_Marthae)* in a minivan with the other cardinals, instead of using a private car. Afterward he went to the guest house where he had resided during the conclave, collected his belongings and insisted on paying the bill.[^cite_note-159]

### Name



Francis in [St. Peter's Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Peter's_Square), two months after his election

At his first audience on 16 March 2013, Francis told journalists that he had chosen the name in honor of [Saint Francis of Assisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_of_Assisi) and had done so because he was especially concerned for the well-being of the poor.[^cite_note-The_Guardian-2013a-160][^cite_note-Vatican_Today-2013-161][^cite_note-162] He explained that, as it was becoming clear during the conclave voting that he would be elected, the Brazilian Cardinal [Cláudio Hummes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cl%C3%A1udio_Hummes) had embraced him and whispered, "Don't forget the poor", which made Bergoglio think of the saint.[^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164] Bergoglio had previously expressed his admiration for St. Francis, explaining that: "He brought to Christianity an idea of poverty against the luxury, pride, vanity of the civil and ecclesiastical powers of the time. He changed history."[^cite_note-bethune-165]

It was the first time that a pope had been named "Francis". On the day of his election, the Vatican clarified that his official papal name was "Francis" and not "Francis I"; that is, no [regnal number](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regnal_number) was used for him. If there is a Francis II, then Francis will be known as Francis I.[^cite_note-Vatican_Today-2013-161][^cite_note-Vatican:_It's_Pope_Francis,_not_Pope_Francis_I-166] It was the first time since [Lando](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Lando)'s 913–914 pontificate that a canonical pope held a name not used by a predecessor.[^cite_note-168]

Francis also said that some cardinal electors had jokingly suggested to him that he should choose either "Adrian", since [Adrian VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrian_VI) had been a reformer of the church, or "Clement", to settle the score with [Clement XIV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clement_XIV) who had [suppressed the Jesuit order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suppression_of_the_Society_of_Jesus).[^cite_note-PapalName-169][^cite_note-170] Bergoglio, had he been elected in 2005, would have chosen the pontifical name of "John XXIV" in honor of [John XXIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_XXIII). He told Cardinal [Francesco Marchisano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Marchisano): "John, I would have called myself John, like the Good Pope; I would have been completely inspired by him."[^cite_note-171]

### Curia



Inauguration of Francis, 19 March 2013

Francis abolished the bonuses paid to Vatican employees upon the election of a new pope, amounting to several million euros, opting instead to donate the money to charity.[^cite_note-172] He also abolished the €25,000 annual bonus paid to cardinals serving on the Board of Supervisors for the [Vatican bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Works_of_Religion).[^cite_note-173]

On 13 April 2013, Francis named eight cardinals to a new [Council of Cardinal Advisers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Cardinal_Advisers) to advise him on revising the organizational structure of the Roman Curia. The group included several known critics of Vatican operations and only one member of the Curia.[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-175]

### Early issues

On the first [Holy Thursday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maundy_Thursday) following his election, Francis washed and kissed the feet of ten male and two female juvenile offenders imprisoned at Rome's Casal del Marmo detention facility, telling them [the ritual of foot washing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maundy_\(foot_washing\)) is a sign that he is at their service.[^cite_note-muslimfeetwashing-176] This was the first time that a pope had included women in this ritual, although he had already done so when he was archbishop.[^cite_note-muslimfeetwashing-176] One of the male and one of the female prisoners were [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim).[^cite_note-muslimfeetwashing-176]

On 31 March 2013, Francis used his first *[Urbi et Orbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbi_et_Orbi)* Easter address to make a plea for [world peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_peace), specifically mentioning the Middle East, Africa, and North and South Korea.[^cite_note-eastersermon-177] He also spoke out against those who give in to "easy gain" in a world filled with greed and made a plea for humanity to become a better guardian of creation by protecting the environment.[^cite_note-eastersermon-177][^cite_note-languages-140] Although the Vatican had prepared greetings in 65 languages, Francis chose not to read them.[^cite_note-languages-140] According to the Vatican, the pope "at least for now, feels at ease using Italian".[^cite_note-178]

In 2013, Francis initially reaffirmed the [Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith)'s programme to [reform the US Leadership Conference of Women Religious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leadership_Conference_of_Women_Religious#Doctrinal_issues)[^cite_note-Uebbing-2013-179] which had been initiated under his predecessor, Pope Benedict XVI. *[The New York Times](30680.md)* reported that the Vatican had formed the opinion in 2012 that the [sisters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_sister_\(Catholic\))' group had some feminist influences, focused too much on ending social and economic injustice and not enough on stopping abortion, and permitted speakers who questioned church doctrine.[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-Uebbing-2013-179] In April 2015 the investigation was brought to a close. While the timing of the closure may have anticipated [a visit by Francis to the US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis's_visit_to_the_United_States) in September 2015, it was noted that the sisters' emphasis is close to that of Francis.[^cite_note-181]

### Synodal church

See also: [Theology of Pope Francis § Decentralization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Decentralization)



Francis in [Quito](209982.md), Ecuador, 2015

Francis oversaw synods on [the family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Extraordinary_General_Assembly_of_the_Synod_of_Bishops) (2014), on [youth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifteenth_Ordinary_General_Assembly_of_the_Synod_of_Bishops) (2018), and on [the church in the Amazon region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synod_of_Bishops_for_the_Pan-Amazon_region) (2019). In 2019 Francis's apostolic constitution *_[Episcopalis communio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopalis_communio)_* allowed that the final document of a synod may become magisterial teaching simply with papal approval. The constitution also allowed for laity to contribute input directly to the synod's secretary general.[^cite_note-sept18-182] Some analysts see the creation of a truly synodal church as likely to become the greatest contribution to Francis's papacy.[^cite_note-183]

On 4 October 2023, Francis convened the beginnings of the [Synod on Synodality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synod_on_Synodality), described by some as the culmination of his papacy and one of the most important events in the Church since the [Second Vatican Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council).[^cite_note-Horowitz-2023-10][^cite_note-Horowitz-2023a-11]

### Institute for the Works of Religion

In the first months of Francis's papacy, the [Institute for the Works of Religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Works_of_Religion), informally known as the Vatican Bank, said that it would become more transparent in its financial dealings.[^cite_note-184] There had long been allegations of corruption and [money laundering](19390.md) connected with the bank.[^cite_note-bank1-185][^cite_note-bank2-186] Francis appointed a commission to advise him about reform of the Bank,[^cite_note-bank1-185][^cite_note-bank2-186] and the finance consulting firm [Promontory Financial Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Promontory_Financial_Group) was assigned to carry out a comprehensive investigation of all customer contacts.[^cite_note-187] In January 2014, Francis replaced four of the five cardinal overseers of the Vatican Bank who had been confirmed in their positions in the final days of Benedict XVI's papacy.[^cite_note-188] Lay experts and clerics were looking into how the bank was run. [Ernst von Freyberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernst_von_Freyberg) was put in charge. [Moneyval](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moneyval) felt more reform was needed, and Francis showed some willingness to close the bank if the reforms proved too difficult.[^cite_note-Jones-189] There was uncertainty about how far reforms could succeed.[^cite_note-190]

### Writings

Main article: [Pope Francis bibliography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis_bibliography)

Pope Francis wrote a variety of books, [encyclicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encyclical), and other texts, including a memoir, *[Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hope_\(Pope_Francis_memoir\))*.[^cite_note-191] On 29 June 2013, Francis published the encyclical *_[Lumen fidei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lumen_fidei)_*, which was largely the work of Benedict XVI but awaited a final draft at his retirement.[^cite_note-192] On 24 November 2013, Francis published his first major letter as pope, the [apostolic exhortation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_exhortation) *_[Evangelii gaudium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelii_gaudium)_*,[^cite_note-193] which he described as the programmatic of his papacy.[^cite_note-National_Catholic_Reporter-2013-194] On 18 June 2015, he published his first own encyclical *_[Laudato si'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laudato_si')_* concerning care for the planet.[^cite_note-195] On 8 April 2016, Francis published his second apostolic exhortation, *[Amoris laetitia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amoris_laetitia)*,[^cite_note-Amoris_laetitia-196] remarking on love within the family. Controversy arose at the end of 2016 when four cardinals formally asked Francis for clarifications, particularly on the issue of giving communion to divorced and civilly remarried Catholics.[^cite_note-197]

A further apostolic exhortation, *[Gaudete et exsultate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaudete_et_exsultate)* (*Rejoice and be glad*), was published on 19 March 2018, dealing with "the [call to holiness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_call_to_holiness)" for all persons. He counters contemporary versions of the [gnostic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gnosticism) and [Pelagian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelagianism) [heresies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heresy_in_Christianity) and describes how Jesus's beatitudes call people to "go against the flow".[^cite_note-198]

In February 2019, Francis acknowledged that priests and bishops were sexually abusing [religious sisters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_sister_\(Catholic\)).[^cite_note-199] He addressed this and the [clergy sex abuse scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clergy_sex_abuse_scandal) by convening a summit on clergy sexual abuse in February 2019.[^cite_note-200] As a follow-up to that summit, on 9 May 2019 Francis [promulgated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Promulgated) the *[motu proprio](793396.md)* *[Vos estis lux mundi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vos_estis_lux_mundi)* which specified responsibilities, including reporting directly to the Holy See on bishops and on one's superior, while simultaneously involving another bishop in the [archdiocese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese) of the accused bishop.[^cite_note-201]

On 4 October 2020, Francis published the encyclical *_[Fratelli tutti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fratelli_tutti)_* on fraternity and social friendship.[^cite_note-Pentin-2020-202]

On 8 December 2020, on the Feast of the [Immaculate Conception](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immaculate_Conception), Pope Francis published the [apostolic letter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecclesiastical_letter#Letters_of_the_popes_in_modern_times) *Patris corde* ("With a Father's Heart").[^cite_note-203] To mark the occasion, the Pope proclaimed a "Year of Saint Joseph" from 8 December 2020 to 8 December 2021 on the 150th Anniversary of the Proclamation of [Saint Joseph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Joseph) as [Patron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patron) of the [Universal Church](606848.md).[^cite_note-204]

On 1 June 2021, Francis published the apostolic constitution *_[Pascite gregem Dei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pascite_gregem_Dei)_*. The document reformed Vatican penal law by strengthening the penalties for sexual abuse and financial crimes; it also more harshly punished the ordination of women.[^cite_note-Giangravé-205]

### Ecumenism and interreligious dialogue

Main article: [Ecumenism and interreligious dialogue of Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecumenism_and_interreligious_dialogue_of_Pope_Francis)



1.5 [Azerbaijani manat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_manat) postage stamp commemorating the pastoral visit of Francis to Azerbaijan on 2 October 2016.

Pope Francis upheld the Second Vatican Council's tradition by promoting [ecumenism](146656.md) with other [Christian denominations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_denominations), encouraging dialogue with other religions, and supporting peace with secular individuals.[^cite_note-206]

### Clerical titles

See also: [Theology of Pope Francis § Clericalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Clericalism)

In January 2014, Francis said that he would appoint fewer [monsignors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monsignor) and only assign those honored to the lowest of the three surviving ranks of monsignor, [chaplain of His Holiness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaplain_of_His_Holiness); it would be awarded only to [diocesan priests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diocesan_priest) at least 65 years old. As archbishop of Buenos Aires, Francis never sought the title for any of his priests. It is believed he associated it with clerical careerism and hierarchy, although he did not apply this restriction to clergy working in the Roman Curia or diplomatic corps where careerism was an even greater concern.[^cite_note-207]

### Canonizations, beatifications and doctors of the church

Main articles: [List of saints canonized by Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_saints_canonized_by_Pope_Francis), [List of people beatified by Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_beatified_by_Pope_Francis), and [List of people declared venerable by Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_declared_venerable_by_Pope_Francis)



Francis on the occasion of the [canonization of John XXIII and John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canonization_of_Pope_John_XXIII_and_Pope_John_Paul_II)

Francis presided over the first canonizations of his pontificate on 12 May 2013 in which he canonized the [Martyrs of Otranto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrs_of_Otranto)—Antonio Primaldo and his 812 companions who had been [executed by the Ottomans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_invasion_of_Otranto) in 1480[^cite_note-208]—as well as the [religious sisters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_sister_\(Catholic\)) [Laura of St. Catherine of Siena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Montoya) and [María Guadalupe García Zavala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mar%C3%ADa_Guadalupe_Garc%C3%ADa_Zavala).[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera-2013-209] In this first canonization, Francis surpassed the [record of Pope John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_saints_canonized_by_Pope_John_Paul_II) in canonizing the most saints in a pontificate.[^cite_note-210]

Saints the Pope canonized include [Louis Martin and Marie-Azélie Guérin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Martin_and_Marie-Az%C3%A9lie_Gu%C3%A9rin) (the first married couple to be named as saints together),[^cite_note-211] [Mother Teresa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_Teresa),[^cite_note-212] and [Óscar Romero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%93scar_Romero).[^cite_note-Pullella-2018-213] Francis canonized three of his predecessors: John XXIII, John Paul II and Paul VI.[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-Pullella-2018-213] Francis also confirmed [Pope John Paul I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_I) to be [Venerable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venerable)[^cite_note-215] and [Blessed](233724.md).[^cite_note-216]

Francis declared two new [Doctors of the Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctors_of_the_Church): [Saint Gregory of Narek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregory_of_Narek) in 2015,[^cite_note-217] and [Saint Irenaeus of Lyon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irenaeus) in 2022.[^cite_note-Esteves2022-218]

### Consistories

Main article: [Cardinals created by Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinals_created_by_Francis)

Francis created 163 cardinals from 76 countries across ten [consistories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_consistory). He held his first consistory in February 2014, a rare occasion in which he publicly appeared with his predecessor, Benedict XVI.[^cite_note-Reuters-219][^cite_note-220] After the 2024 consistory, 110 cardinals appointed by Francis were under the age of 80 and thus eligible to vote at a [papal conclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_conclave). There were, at that point, 110 cardinal-electors created by Francis, 24 created by Benedict XVI, and six created by John Paul II.[^cite_note-221]

Francis's appointments made the [College of Cardinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Cardinals) less European-dominated.[^cite_note-Diamant-222] He appointed many cardinals from [developing countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developing_countries), including some of the world's poorest, and from countries on the peripheries of the church.[^cite_note-Reese2023Conclave-223]

Compared to his predecessors, Francis made fewer appointments of [Roman Curia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Curia) officials to the cardinalate.[^cite_note-Reese2023Conclave-223] This was part of a general trend under Francis to a more decentralized church.[^cite_note-224] Compared to his predecessor Benedict, who preferred to appoint academically inclined churchmen as cardinal, Francis favored cardinals with a more pastoral focus,[^cite_note-Reese2023Conclave-223] especially those known for a focus on the poor and marginalized.[^cite_note-225] Francis also dropped the traditional custom of always appointing the archbishops of certain historically prominent [sees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_see) (such as the [Patriarch of Venice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriarch_of_Venice) and [Archbishop of Milan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Milan)) as cardinals.[^cite_note-Reese2023Conclave-223]

### Year of Mercy

Main article: [Extraordinary Jubilee of Mercy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extraordinary_Jubilee_of_Mercy)



Francis opens the [Holy Door](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_door), marking the beginning of the [Extraordinary Jubilee of Mercy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extraordinary_Jubilee_of_Mercy)

In his April 2015 [papal bull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_bull) of indiction, *Misericordiae Vultus* (*The Face of [Mercy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercy)*), Francis inaugurated a Special [Jubilee Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jubilee_\(Christianity\)) of Mercy to run from 8 December 2015, [Solemnity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solemnity) of the [Immaculate Conception](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immaculate_Conception) of the [Blessed Virgin Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blessed_Virgin_Mary), to the last Sunday before [Advent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advent) and the Solemnity of the [Feast of Christ the King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feast_of_Christ_the_King) of the Universe on 20 November 2016.[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227]

The [Holy Doors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Door) of the major basilicas of Rome were opened, and special "Doors of Mercy" were opened at cathedrals and other major churches around the world, where the faithful could earn indulgences by fulfilling the usual conditions of prayer for the pope's intentions, confession, and detachment from sin, and communion.[^cite_note-opening-228][^cite_note-229] During Lent of that year, special 24-hour penance services were celebrated, and during the year, special qualified and experienced priests called "Missionaries of Mercy" were available in every diocese to forgive even severe, special-case sins normally reserved to the Holy See's [Apostolic Penitentiary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_Penitentiary).[^cite_note-Misericordiae-230][^cite_note-time-231]

Francis established the [World Day of the Poor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Day_of_the_Poor) in his Apostolic letter, *_Misericordia et Misera_*, issued on 20 November 2016 to celebrate the end of the Extraordinary Jubilee of Mercy.[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233]

### COVID-19 pandemic

See also: [COVID-19 pandemic in Vatican City](63299650.md)

During the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), Francis canceled [his regular general audiences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_audience) at St. Peter's Square to keep crowds from gathering and spreading the virus, which had [seriously affected Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Italy).[^cite_note-234] He encouraged priests to visit patients and health workers;[^cite_note-235] urged the faithful not to forget the poor during the time of crisis;[^cite_note-236] offered prayers for people with the virus in China;[^cite_note-237] and invoked the Blessed Virgin Mary under her title [Salus Populi Romani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salus_Populi_Romani), as the [Diocese of Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diocese_of_Rome) observed a period of prayer and fasting in recognition of the victims.[^cite_note-238] The pontiff reacted with displeasure on 13 March 2020 to the news that the Vicar General had closed all churches in the Diocese of Rome. Despite [Italy being under a quarantine lockdown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Italy_coronavirus_lockdown), Francis pleaded "not to leave the ... people alone" and worked to partially reverse the closures.[^cite_note-239]

On 20 March 2020, Francis asked the [Dicastery for Promoting Integral Human Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dicastery_for_Promoting_Integral_Human_Development) (DPIHD) to create a [Vatican COVID-19 Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vatican_COVID-19_Commission) to listen to concerns and develop responses for the future.[^cite_note-240][^cite_note-241] On 27 March, Francis gave an extraordinary benediction *[Urbi et Orbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbi_et_Orbi)*.[^cite_note-242] In his homily on [calming the storm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calming_the_storm) in the [Gospel of Mark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel_of_Mark), Francis described the setting:

> Dense darkness has thickened on our squares, streets and cities; it looks over our lives filling everything with a deafening silence and a desolate void that paralyzes everything in its passage: you can feel it in the air, you can feel it in your gestures. ...In the face of suffering, where the true development of our peoples is measured, we discover and experience the priestly prayer of Jesus: 'may all be one'.[^cite_note-243]

Francis maintained that getting [COVID-19 vaccination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_vaccination) was a moral obligation.[^cite_note-244] In response to the [economic harm caused by the COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_impact_of_the_COVID-19_pandemic), Francis said that it was the time to consider implementing a [universal basic wage](28310828.md).[^cite_note-245]

### Death penalty

Francis committed the [Catholic Church to support worldwide abolition of the death penalty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_and_capital_punishment).[^cite_note-Brockhaus-2018-246] In 2018, Francis revised the *[Catechism of the Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catechism_of_the_Catholic_Church)* to read that "in the light of the [Gospel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel)" the death penalty is "inadmissible because it is an attack on the inviolability and [dignity of the person](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_dignity)" and that the Catholic Church "works with determination for its abolition worldwide".[^cite_note-Brockhaus-2018-246][^cite_note-Harlan-2018-247] In his 2020 encyclical *_[Fratelli tutti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fratelli_tutti)_*, Francis repeated that the death penalty was "inadmissible", and that "there can be no stepping back from this position."[^cite_note-Pentin-2020-202]

On 9 January 2022, Francis stated in his annual speech to Vatican ambassadors: "The death penalty cannot be employed for a purported state justice, since it does not constitute a deterrent nor render justice to victims, but only fuels the thirst for vengeance."[^cite_note-Pullella-2023-248]

### Role of women

See also: [Women in the Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_the_Catholic_Church)

Francis categorically rejected the [ordination of women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordination_of_women) as priests.[^cite_note-Horowitz-2023-10] Early in his papacy, he initiated dialogue on the possibility of [deaconesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deaconesses), creating in 2016 a [Study Commission on the Women's Diaconate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Study_Commission_on_the_Women's_Diaconate) to research the role of female deacons in early Christianity.[^cite_note-249] Its report was not made public,[^cite_note-Dulle-250] but Francis said in 2019 that the commission was unable to come to a consensus.[^cite_note-SplitRpt-251] In April 2020, Francis empaneled a new commission, led by Cardinal [Giuseppe Petrocchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Petrocchi) with a new membership, to study the issue.[^cite_note-252] Francis delayed a decision on the issue for several years.[^cite_note-Horowitz-2023-10] In interviews in late 2023 and 2024, he appeared to reject the idea of women deacons, saying that "[holy orders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_orders_in_the_Catholic_Church) is reserved for men."[^cite_note-CNA2023-253][^cite_note-Dulle-250] Francis said that "the fact that the woman does not access ministerial life is not a deprivation, because her place is much more important"[^cite_note-CNA2023-253] and that women had a [charism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charism) separate from "the ministerial way."[^cite_note-Dulle-250]

In January 2021, Francis issued [Spiritus Domini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spiritus_Domini_\(Pope_Francis\)), allowing bishops to institute women to the ministries of [acolyte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acolyte) and [lector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reader_\(liturgy\)). While these instituted ministries were previously reserved to men, Catholic women already carried out these duties without institution in most of the world. Francis wrote that these [ministries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_ministry) are fundamentally distinct from those reserved to ordained clergy.[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-Mares-2021-255][^cite_note-256] The following month, Francis appointed women to several positions previously held only by men: a French member of the [Xaviere Missionary Sisters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregation_of_Xavi%C3%A8res), [Nathalie Becquart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nathalie_Becquart), was appointed co-undersecretary of the [Synod of Bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synod_of_Bishops_in_the_Catholic_Church), and Italian magistrate [Catia Summaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catia_Summaria) became the first woman Promoter of Justice in the Vatican's Court of Appeals.[^cite_note-NBC_News-2021-257]

In April 2023, Francis announced that 35 women would be allowed to vote at the [Sixteenth Ordinary General Assembly of the Synod of Bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixteenth_Ordinary_General_Assembly_of_the_Synod_of_Bishops) ("just over 10%" of all voters),[^cite_note-258] marking the first time women are allowed to vote at any Catholic Synod of Bishops.[^cite_note-Pullella-2023b-259]

### Financial corruption

Francis was mandated by electing cardinals to sort out Vatican finances following scandals during the papacies of Pope Benedict and Pope John Paul II. He stated he was determined to end corruption in the Catholic Church but was not very optimistic due to it being a human problem dating back centuries.[^cite_note-260]

### Apologies toward Indigenous peoples

Early in 2022, Francis expressed "shame and sorrow" for the Catholic Church's role in abuses against the [Indigenous peoples in Canada](196075.md).[^cite_note-Neuman2022-261][^cite_note-Horowitz-2022-262] Later, in July 2022, Francis [made an apostolic journey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visit_by_Pope_Francis_to_Canada) to [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Canada), where he expressed sorrow, indignation, and shame over the church's abuse of Canadian Indigenous children in [residential schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Indian_residential_school_system).[^cite_note-CNN-2022-263] Francis described the [Canadian Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Canada)'s role as a "[cultural genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_genocide)".[^cite_note-Horowitz-2022-262] He apologized for the church's role in "projects of cultural destruction" and forced [assimilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_assimilation_of_indigenous_peoples).[^cite_note-CNN-2022-263] Near the former Ermineskin Indian Residential School, the site of a search for [unmarked graves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Indian_residential_school_gravesites), Francis said: "I humbly beg forgiveness for the evil committed by so many Christians against the Indigenous peoples."[^cite_note-Neuman2022-261] He visited the [Ermineskin Cree Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ermineskin_Cree_Nation)'s cemetery at its [Maskwacis reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ermineskin_138).[^cite_note-Neuman2022-261] Francis promised a serious investigation into the history of abuse.[^cite_note-CNN-2022-263]

### Sexual abuse response

Main article: [Catholic Church sexual abuse cases](158934.md)

In 2010, then-Cardinal Bergoglio commissioned a study which concluded that Father [Julio César Grassi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julio_C%C3%A9sar_Grassi), a priest convicted of child sexual abuse, was innocent, that his victims were lying, and that the case against him never should have gone to trial.[^cite_note-study-264] However, the [Supreme Court of Argentina](2800711.md) upheld the conviction and prison sentence against Grassi in March 2017.[^cite_note-study-264]

Early in his papacy, Francis chose a more lenient sentence for Mauro Inzoli, an Italian priest accused of child sexual abuse.[^cite_note-Ide2017-265] A church tribunal had ruled that Inzoli should be [laicized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loss_of_clerical_state) (defrocked),[^cite_note-Pullella2017Admit-266] and he was defrocked in 2012 by Francis's predecessor Benedict.[^cite_note-Ide2017-265] Francis, however, reversed this decision in 2014; Francis agreed with the [bishop of Crema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishop_of_Crema) that Inzoli should remain a priest but be removed from public ministry and ordered to retire to "a life of prayer and humble discretion."[^cite_note-Ide2017-265][^cite_note-Pullella2017Admit-266] Inzoli was convicted of sexually abusing children in Italian civil court in 2016, and sentenced to prison.[^cite_note-Ide2017-265] In unscripted remarks to the [Pontifical Commission for the Protection of Minors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontifical_Commission_for_the_Protection_of_Minors) in September 2017, Francis admitted that he mishandled the Inzoli case, saying that as a new pope, "I did not understand these things well and chose the more benevolent of the two sentences but after two years the priest had a relapse. I learned from this."[^cite_note-Pullella2017Admit-266] In the same remarks, he commented that the church "arrived late" in dealing with sexual abuse cases.[^cite_note-Pullella2017Admit-266]

In 2015, Francis was criticized for supporting Chilean bishop [Juan Barros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Barros) who was accused of covering up [Catholic Church sexual abuse cases in Chile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_sexual_abuse_cases_in_Chile), including crimes committed against minors.[^cite_note-267] In 2018, Francis acknowledged he had made "grave errors" in judgment about Barros, apologized to the victims and launched a Vatican investigation that resulted in the resignation of three Chilean bishops: Barros, [Gonzalo Duarte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gonzalo_Duarte_Garc%C3%ADa_de_Cort%C3%A1zar), and [Cristián Caro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristi%C3%A1n_Caro_Cordero).[^cite_note-268]

In 2019, Francis defrocked [Theodore McCarrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodore_McCarrick), a former [archbishop of Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Washington), who maintained a prominent position in the church for decades despite repeated reports of sexual misconduct against him dating back to the 1980s.
