In 2017, after renewed allegations against McCarrick, Francis commissioned a Vatican investigation, which found that McCarrick had sexually molested both adults and minors.[^cite_note-WinfieldNov2020-269] In July 2018, McCarrick resigned from the [College of Cardinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Cardinals); in October 2018, Francis ordered a review of the Church's "institutional knowledge and decision-making" related to McCarrick.[^cite_note-McCarrickReport-270] Francis authorized the release, in November 2020, of the report of the Vatican's two-year investigation into McCarrick's career.[^cite_note-McCarrickReport-270][^cite_note-WinfieldNov2020-269] The report largely faulted [Pope John Paul II](23805.md), who appointed McCarrick as archbishop in 2000 and accepted the churchman's denials of sexual abuse, despite multiple reports.[^cite_note-WinfieldNov2020-269] The report also found that [Pope Benedict XVI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI) placed informal restrictions on McCarrick, but these were never enforced, and Benedict did not investigate or formally sanction McCarrick even after he disregarded those restrictions.[^cite_note-WinfieldNov2020-269] The report concluded that Francis, before 2017, "had heard only that there had been allegations and rumors related to immoral conduct with adults occurring prior to McCarrick's appointment to Washington" and continued the approach of his predecessors John Paul and Benedict.[^cite_note-WinfieldNov2020-269]

Francis convened a summit on sexual abuse in February 2019, organized by [Hans Zollner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Zollner); some abuse survivors expressed disappointment that the summit did not result in concrete rules on abuse prevention, responses to abuse, and Church cooperation with law enforcement authorities.[^cite_note-271] In December 2019, Francis abolished the "[pontifical secrecy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontifical_secrecy)" privilege in sexual abuse cases, clarifying that bishops do not need authorization from the Vatican to turn over materials from canonical trials upon request of civil law enforcement authorities.[^cite_note-Giangravé-205][^cite_note-Chakraborty-272] The lifting of the confidentiality rule was praised by victim advocates, but did not require the Church to affirmatively turn over canonical documents to civil authorities.[^cite_note-Giangravé-205][^cite_note-Chakraborty-272]

In November 2021, Francis thanked journalists for their work in uncovering child sexual abuse scandals in the church. He also thanked journalists for "helping us not to sweep it under the carpet, and for the voice you have given to the abuse victims".[^cite_note-273]

In November 2022, French Cardinal [Jean-Pierre Ricard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Pierre_Ricard) admitted to having sexually abused a 14-year-old girl in the 1980s in [Marseille](40888948.md).[^cite_note-274] French authorities opened an investigation into the case while Francis commented that now that "everything is clearer \[...] more cases like this shouldn't surprise \[anyone]", and condemned sexual abuse as "against priestly nature, and also against social nature".[^cite_note-275][^cite_note-276] Francis did not deprive Ricard of his status and privileges as a cardinal.[^cite_note-277]

[Francis visited Ireland in 2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis's_visit_to_Ireland), marking the first papal tour of the country since [John Paul II's historic trip in 1979](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visit_by_Pope_John_Paul_II_to_Ireland).[^cite_note-278] He has apologized for sexual abuses by clergy in the United States and Ireland.[^cite_note-279]

The case of [Slovenian](27338.md) priest [Marko Rupnik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marko_Rupnik), accused of psychological, spiritual, and sexual abuse against multiple women, including nuns, drew significant controversy due to the Vatican's handling of the allegations.[^cite_note-The_Pillar-2022-280] Initially, the [Dicastery for the Doctrine of the Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dicastery_for_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith) (DDF) declined to prosecute, citing the statute of limitations, despite acknowledging there was a case to answer.[^cite_note-281] Rupnik was expelled from the Society of Jesus in 2023 for disobedience rather than for the abuse allegations and was later incardinated into the [Diocese of Koper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Koper). Following widespread public outcry, Pope Francis ordered the case to be reopened and re-examined. After his conviction, Rupnik preached in 2020 a [Lenten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lent) meditation for priests working in the [Roman Curia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Curia), including Pope Francis and [Luis Ladaria Ferrer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Ladaria_Ferrer), and met privately with Pope Francis in January 2022.[^cite_note-The_Pillar-2022-280] Criticism intensified after it was revealed that artwork by Rupnik remains in use by the Vatican, including in Pope Francis's personal residence, despite calls from Cardinal [Seán Patrick O'Malley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Se%C3%A1n_Patrick_O'Malley), head of the Pontifical Commission for the Protection of Minors, for its removal. The controversy fueled broader scrutiny of the Vatican's response to clergy abuse cases and its commitment to transparency and justice.[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284] In January 2025, Cardinal [Víctor Manuel Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C3%ADctor_Manuel_Fern%C3%A1ndez) confirmed the DDF was working to establish an independent tribunal to move forward with judicial proceedings.[^cite_note-285]

## Theological emphases and teachings

Main article: [Theology of Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis)

In *_Evangelii gaudium_*, Francis revealed what would be the emphases of his pontificate: a missionary impulse among all Catholics, sharing the faith more actively, avoiding worldliness by more visibly living the gospel of God's mercy, and helping the poor and working for [social justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_social_teaching).[^cite_note-286]

Since 2016, criticism against Francis by theological conservatives has intensified.[^cite_note-293] Some have explained the level of disagreement as due to his going beyond theoretical principles to pastoral discernment.[^cite_note-294]

### Evangelization

See also: [Theology of Pope Francis § The Church's mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#The_Church's_mission)

From his first major letter *_[Evangelii gaudium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelii_gaudium)_* (*Joy to the World*), Francis called for "a missionary and pastoral conversion" whereby the laity would fully share in the missionary task of the church.[^cite_note-National_Catholic_Reporter-2013-194][^cite_note-295] Then, in his letter on the call of all to the same holiness, *_[Gaudete et exsultate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaudete_et_exsultate)_*, Francis describes holiness as "an impulse to evangelize and to leave a mark in this world".[^cite_note-296]

### Church governance

See also: [Theology of Pope Francis § Church governance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Church_governance)



A Carnival float of Francis and Germany's prelate [Franz-Peter Tebartz-van Elst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franz-Peter_Tebartz-van_Elst), Düsseldorf, 2014

Francis called for decentralization of governance away from Rome and for a synodal manner of decision-making in dialogue with the people.[^cite_note-297] He strongly opposed [clericalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Clericalism)[^cite_note-298] and made [women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Roles_for_women) full members of the church's dicasteries in Rome.[^cite_note-299]

### Environment and climate change

See also: [Theology of Pope Francis § Environment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Environment)

Francis's naming was an early indication of how he shared Francis of Assisi's care for all of creation. This was followed in May 2015 with his major [encyclical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encyclical) on the environment, *_[Laudato si'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laudato_si')_* (*Praise be to you*).[^cite_note-300] In October 2023, in advance of the [2023 United Nations Climate Change Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_United_Nations_Climate_Change_Conference) (COP28), Francis issued the [apostolic exhortation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_exhortation) *_[Laudate Deum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laudate_Deum)_* (*Praise God*), in which he called for decisive action to against the [climate crisis](17987598.md) and condemned [climate change denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_denial).[^cite_note-katholisch.de-301][^cite_note-Povoledo-2023-302]

At the 2017 [World Food Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Food_Day) ceremony, Francis highlighted the daily impacts of climate change and the solutions provided by scientific knowledge. He pointed out that while the international community had established legal frameworks such as the Paris Agreement, some nations had been withdrawing. He then expressed concern over a renewed indifference to ecosystem balance, the belief in controlling limited resources, and a greed for profit.[^cite_note-Vatican_FAO_20171016-303] In 2019, he stated that [ecocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecocide) was a sin and should be made "a fifth category of crimes against peace".[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306]

In May 2024, Francis organized a climate summit that issued a Planetary Protocol for Climate Change Resilience including three pillars: greenhouse gas emissions reduction (while prioritizing [nature-based solutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature-based_solutions)), [climate change adaptation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_adaptation), and societal transformation.[^cite_note-307] The next month, Francis issued an apostolic letter titled *_Fratello sole_* (*Brother sun*, referring to Saint Francis' [Canticle of the Sun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canticle_of_the_Sun)), ordering the Vatican to construct an [agrivoltaics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agrivoltaics) facility on its land holdings on the outskirts of Rome, as a gesture of the Church towards the environmental movement.[^cite_note-Fratello_Sole_English_text-308]

### Option for the poor

See also: [Theology of Pope Francis § Option for the poor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Option_for_the_poor), [Theology of Pope Francis § Capitalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Capitalism), and [Theology of Pope Francis § Liberation theology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Liberation_theology)



Francis visits a [favela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Favela) in Brazil during [World Youth Day 2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Youth_Day_2013)

Francis had highly extolled "popular movements" which demonstrate the "strength of us", serve as a remedy to the "culture of the self", and are based on solidarity with the poor and the common good.[^cite_note-309] He had praised liberation theology founder [Gustavo Gutierrez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustavo_Gutierrez).[^cite_note-310] In 2024, he met with representatives of the Dialop group, a discussion group between Christians and [Marxists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxists), and encouraged them to cooperate.[^cite_note-311]

In September 2024, Francis renewed calls for a universal basic income, as well as higher taxes on billionaires.[^cite_note-312]

### Morality

See also: [Theology of Pope Francis § Morality as a vehicle of God's mercy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Morality_as_a_vehicle_of_God's_mercy), [Theology of Pope Francis § Sexual morality as Good News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Sexual_morality_as_Good_News), and [Pope Francis and LGBTQ topics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis_and_LGBTQ_topics)

Cardinal [Walter Kasper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_Kasper) had called mercy "the key word of his pontificate".[^cite_note-Ivereigh-2015-53]<sup>: 31–32 </sup> His papal motto *_Miserando atque eligendo_* ("by having mercy and by choosing") contains a central theme of his papacy, God's mercy.[^cite_note-web.archive.org2-313][^cite_note-314] While maintaining the Catholic Church's traditional teaching against abortion, Francis had referred to the "obsession" of some Catholics with a few issues such as "abortion, gay marriage and the use of contraceptive methods" which "do not show the heart of the message of Jesus Christ".[^cite_note-Spadaro-2013-315]

### LGBTQ

Main article: [Pope Francis and LGBTQ topics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis_and_LGBTQ_topics)

While serving as the [archbishop of Buenos Aires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Buenos_Aires), Bergoglio led public opposition to the parliamentary bill on legalizing [same-sex marriage in Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Argentina), which was eventually approved by the [Argentine Senate](751152.md) in 2010.[^cite_note-316] A letter he wrote in that campaign was criticized for using "medieval" and "obscurantist" language.[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319] A church source quoted in the Argentine newspaper *[La Nación](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Naci%C3%B3n)* called the letter a strategic error that contributed to the bill's success.[^cite_note-320]

As Pope, Francis marked a more accommodative tone on some [LGBTQ topics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outline_of_LGBTQ_topics) than his predecessors.[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322] In July 2013, his televised "Who am I to judge?" statement was widely reported in the international press, becoming one of his most famous statements on [LGBTQ](78174325.md) people.[^cite_note-Pullella-2016-323][^cite_note-Pullella-2023a-324][^cite_note-325] In other public statements, Francis emphasized the need to accept, welcome, and accompany LGBTQ people,[^cite_note-Spadaro-2013-315][^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327] including LGBTQ children.[^cite_note-328][^cite_note-Catholic_News_Agency-2020-329] Francis reiterated traditional Catholic teaching that [marriage](395673.md) is between a man and a woman,[^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331] but supported [civil unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_union) as legal protections for [same-sex couples](19771642.md).[^cite_note-Catholic_News_Agency-2020-329][^cite_note-332] Under his pontificate, the [Dicastery for the Doctrine of the Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dicastery_for_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith) confirmed that transgender people can be baptised.[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334] The blessing of individuals in same-sex relationships was allowed by the document *[Fiducia supplicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiducia_supplicans)*.[^cite_note-vatican.va-335] Francis privately met with many LGBTQ people and activists.[^cite_note-336] In 2013, Francis was named Person of the Year by *[The Advocate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Advocate_\(magazine\))*, an American [LGBTQ magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_LGBTQ_periodicals).[^cite_note-337]

In September 2015, Francis met with [Kim Davis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Davis), a [county clerk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_clerk) who was jailed for six days for [contempt of court](7201.md) for refusing to issue [marriage licenses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marriage_license) for same-sex couples.[^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339] In August 2018, Francis was criticized for suggesting that gay children seek [psychiatric treatment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychiatry), but he then rescinded the statement.[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341] He described [gender theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_studies) and children's education on [gender-affirming surgery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender-affirming_surgery) as "ideological colonization".[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-Pullella-2016-323] He also criticized "[gender ideology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_ideology)", yet said that the 1907 novel *[Lord of the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lord_of_the_World)* depicted what he was intending to criticize.[^cite_note-DignitasGramick-343] He further wrote:

> Gender ideology is something other than homosexual or transsexual people. Gender ideology makes everyone equal without respect for personal history. I understand the concern about that paragraph in *Dignitas Infinita*, but it\[*[sic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic)*] refers not to transgender people but to gender ideology, which nullifies differences. Transgender people must be accepted and integrated into society.[^cite_note-DignitasGramick-343]

In a January 2023 interview with the [Associated Press](18935732.md), Francis denounced the [criminalization of homosexuality](69713049.md) (which he called "unjust"); he also called on the Catholic Church to "distinguish between a sin and crime" and asked bishops supporting such laws to reverse their position.[^cite_note-Staff-2023-17][^cite_note-Pullella-2023a-324][^cite_note-344] Francis repeated this stance the following month.[^cite_note-Pullella-2023a-324]

### International policy

Francis had regularly been accused by conservatives of having a "soft spot" for leftist populist movements.[^cite_note-345]



Francis with [Raúl Castro](221749.md), 2015

After Francis's visit to Cuba in 2015, Catholic Yale historian [Carlos Eire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Eire) said Francis had a "[preferential option](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Option_for_the_poor) for the oppressors" in Cuba.[^cite_note-346] Francis had expressed criticism towards [right-wing populism](8354382.md).[^cite_note-347] Since 2016, Francis had been contrasted with US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md),[^cite_note-348][^cite_note-Lerer-2025-16] with some conservative critics drawing comparisons between the two.[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350] During the [2016 United States presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election), Francis said of Trump, "A person who only thinks about building walls, wherever they may be, and not building bridges, is not Christian." Trump responded, "For a religious leader to question a person's faith is disgraceful."[^cite_note-Lauter-2016-351]

In response to criticism from [Venezuela's bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_Conference_of_Venezuela), President [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) said in 2017 that he had the support of Francis.[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353] Francis met with the country's bishops in June 2017, and the Venezuelan bishops' conference president stated, "There is no distance between the episcopal conference and the Holy See."[^cite_note-354] In January 2019, 20 former presidents in Latin America wrote a letter to Francis criticizing his Christmas address regarding the ongoing [Venezuelan crisis](52966258.md) for being too simplistic and for not acknowledging what they believed to be the causes of the suffering of the victims of the crisis.[^cite_note-355] Francis had sought peace in the crisis without picking a side.[^cite_note-356]

#### Position toward China

Francis took a more conciliatory approach toward the [People's Republic of China](5405.md) than any previous pope.[^cite_note-Rocca2024-357] He continued the [Vatican's longstanding diplomatic recognition of the Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_See%E2%80%93Taiwan_relations) ([Taiwan](25734.md)), [rather than the People's Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One-China_policy); Vatican City is one of [just 12 countries to formally recognize Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Taiwan).[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359] In 2018, however, Francis approved a [provisional Vatican-China agreement](21396316.md#2018_Holy_See%E2%80%93China_agreement) intended to normalize the situation of [China's Catholics](1714754.md) who numbered approximately 10 million as of 2024.[^cite_note-Rocca2024-357] Before, the Chinese government claimed the [authority to appoint bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appointment_of_Catholic_bishops), without papal approval, through the state-controlled [Chinese Catholic Patriotic Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Catholic_Patriotic_Association), in contravention of longstanding church doctrine.[^cite_note-Rocca2024-357] Under the 2018 agreement, the Vatican consults with the Chinese government on the appointment of bishops and pledged not to appoint any bishop in China without Beijing's approval;[^cite_note-Rocca2024-357] in return, the Chinese government recognized the pope as the supreme head of the Catholic Church.[^cite_note-Pullella2022Church-360] The agreement was renewed for two years in 2020.[^cite_note-MaresSantucci2023-361][^cite_note-Pullella2022Church-360]

Francis's efforts toward rapprochement with China were highly controversial; a leading critic, Cardinal [Joseph Zen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Zen), said the 2018 agreement was a step toward the "annihilation" of the Catholic Church in China.[^cite_note-Rocca2024-357][^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] Critics said that the 2018 agreement "sold out" Chinese Catholics by accepting infringements on [religious freedom](46006.md), undermining the Vatican's spiritual authority.[^cite_note-Pullella2022Church-360][^cite_note-Rocca2024-357]

The Chinese government repeatedly violated the 2018 deal with the Vatican.[^cite_note-Pullella2022Church-360][^cite_note-364] Francis had defended the Vatican's dialogue with China on the appointment of new bishops, saying in 2021 that uneasy dialogue was better than no dialogue at all.[^cite_note-365] From the signing of the agreement until 2022, only six Catholic bishops in China were appointed.[^cite_note-Pullella2022Church-360] In November 2022, the Vatican publicly accused China of violating the agreement by installing [John Peng Weizhao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Peng_Weizhao) as an [auxiliary bishop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auxiliary_bishop) without Vatican approval.[^cite_note-Pullella2022Church-360] In April 2023, the Chinese government also installed [Joseph Shen Bin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Shen_Bin) as [bishop of Shanghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Shanghai) without Vatican approval. Three months later, Francis recognized Shen Bin's appointment; the Vatican secretary of state, [Pietro Parolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pietro_Parolin), said that the pope wanted to "remedy the [canonical irregularity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canonical_irregularity) created in Shanghai, in view of the greater good of the diocese and the fruitful exercise of the bishop's pastoral ministry."[^cite_note-MaresSantucci2023-361] The Vatican and the Chinese government renewed the agreement in 2022 and again in 2024.[^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367]

In November 2020, Francis named China's [Uyghur](31783.md) minority among a list of the world's persecuted peoples. He wrote: "I think often of persecuted peoples: the [Rohingya](1918632.md) \[Muslims in Myanmar], the poor [Uighurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Uyghurs_in_China), the [Yazidi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yazidi)—what [ISIS](9087364.md) did to them was truly cruel—or [Christians in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christians_in_Egypt) and [Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Pakistan) killed by bombs that went off while they prayed in church." [Zhao Lijian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhao_Lijian), the spokesman of the Foreign Ministry of China, said Francis's remarks had "no factual basis".[^cite_note-368]

In 2019, during the [Hong Kong pro-democracy protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932020_Hong_Kong_protests), Francis was criticized by Zen and other Catholic clergy in Hong Kong for failing to take a stand against China's repression and instead being quoted as saying, "I would like to go to China. I love China." Francis compared the protests in Hong Kong to those seen [in Chile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932021_Chilean_protests) and [in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_vests_protests).[^cite_note-369]

### Theological disagreements

#### *Amoris laetitia*

On a theological level, controversy arose after the publication of the apostolic exhortation *_[Amoris laetitia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amoris_laetitia)_*, especially regarding whether the exhortation had changed the Catholic Church's sacramental discipline concerning access to the [sacraments of Penance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacrament_of_Penance) and the [Eucharist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eucharist_in_the_Catholic_Church) for divorced couples who have [civilly remarried](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_marriage).[^cite_note-Oullet-370] Francis had written: "It is important that the divorced who have entered a new union should be made to feel part of the Church." He called for "a responsible personal and pastoral discernment of particular cases". He went on to say: "It is true that general rules set forth a good which can never be disregarded or neglected, but in their formulation they cannot provide absolutely for all particular situations."[^cite_note-Carroll-371]

The exhortation had been implemented in different ways by bishops around the world.[^cite_note-372] In July 2017, a group of conservative clergy, academics, and laymen signed a document labeled as a "[Filial Correction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Correctio_filialis_de_haeresibus_propagatis)" of Francis,[^cite_note-cnn-373] which criticized the Pope for promoting what it described as seven heretical propositions through various words, actions, and omissions during his pontificate.[^cite_note-Correctio_filialis_de_haeresibus_propagatis-374]

#### *Document on Human Fraternity*

The *[Document on Human Fraternity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Document_on_Human_Fraternity) for World Peace and Living Together* is a joint statement signed by Francis and [Ahmed el-Tayeb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_el-Tayeb), [Grand Imam of Al-Azhar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Imam_of_Al-Azhar), on 4 February 2019. This statement is concerned with how different faiths can live peaceably in the same areas; it later inspired the [International Day of Human Fraternity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Day_of_Human_Fraternity), as acknowledged by the UN Secretary-General [António Guterres](471806.md).[^cite_note-first-ever-375][^cite_note-376] Criticisms focused particularly on the passage about God's will with regard to the diversity of religions, claiming that the "pluralism and the diversity of religions, colour, sex, race and language are willed by God in His wisdom, through which He created human beings".[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-Reynolds-2019-378] Catholic theologian Chad Pecknold wrote that this sentence was "puzzling, and potentially problematic".[^cite_note-Farrow-2019-379] Some Catholic observers tried to understand it as an allusion to the "permissive will" of God, allowing evil on earth.[^cite_note-Reynolds-2019-378] Pecknold wrote that the diversity of religions might also be "evidence of our natural desire to know God".[^cite_note-Farrow-2019-379] Bishop [Athanasius Schneider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athanasius_Schneider) claims that Pope Francis clarified to him that he was referring to "the permissive will of God".[^cite_note-380]

#### *Traditionis custodes* and the Tridentine Mass

In July 2021, Francis issued *_[motu proprio](793396.md)_*, the [apostolic letter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecclesiastical_letter#Letters_of_the_popes_in_modern_times) titled *_[Traditionis custodes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditionis_custodes)_*, which reversed the decision of his predecessor Benedict XVI in *_[Summorum Pontificum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summorum_Pontificum)_* and imposed new restrictions on the use of the [Traditional Latin Mass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tridentine_Mass). The letter returned to the bishops the power to grant or ban the Latin Mass in their dioceses, and required newly ordained priests to request permission before performing the old rite, among other changes.[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-Horowitz-2021-382] *_Traditionis custodes_* had been criticized by prelates including cardinals [Raymond Leo Burke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raymond_Leo_Burke), [Gerhard Ludwig Müller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerhard_Ludwig_M%C3%BCller), and [Joseph Zen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Zen), and many lay faithful. Edwin Pentin wrote in *[National Catholic Register](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Catholic_Register)* that "The most general criticism is that the restrictions are unnecessary, needlessly harsh, and implemented in an unjustifiably swift fashion."[^cite_note-383]

#### *Fiducia supplicans*

In December 2023, the [Dicastery for the Doctrine of the Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dicastery_for_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith) issued a declaration, *_[Fiducia supplicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiducia_supplicans)_*, approved by Francis.[^cite_note-vatican.va-335] *_Fiducia supplicans_* intended to provide clarification and reforms on the Catholic Church's treatment of "irregular relationships", defined as those who establish a [monogamous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monogamy) and emotional bond that lasts over time and have not contracted a Catholic marriage. Notably, it allows [Catholic priests](8655005.md) to perform "spontaneous blessings" of same-sex couples, as well as opposite-sex couples who are not married, and civilly married couples at least one party of which was previously divorced but had not received an [annulment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annulment_\(Catholic_Church\)).[^cite_note-384]

*_Fiducia supplicans_* sparked considerable controversy among Catholics, including from several conservative commentators, clerical congregations, and high-profile cardinals, bishops, priests, and lay people.[^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] Cardinal [Gerhard Ludwig Müller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerhard_Ludwig_M%C3%BCller) called it "sacrilegious and blasphemous" and "self-contradictory".[^cite_note-388] Cardinal [Robert Sarah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Sarah) described the blessing of couples in irregular situations as "a heresy that seriously undermines the Church".[^cite_note-389] On 11 January 2024, Cardinal [Fridolin Ambongo Besungu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fridolin_Ambongo_Besungu) announced that all episcopal conferences in Africa, represented in [SECAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symposium_of_Episcopal_Conferences_of_Africa_and_Madagascar), would reject blessings for same-sex couples, stating that "the extra-liturgical blessings proposed in the declaration...cannot be carried out in Africa without exposing themselves to scandals".[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391]

## International diplomatic role



Map indicating [countries visited by Francis as pope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pastoral_visits_of_Pope_Francis_outside_Italy)



Pope Francis with Italian president [Sergio Mattarella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Mattarella) in 2022

### Cuba

When Francis was archbishop of Buenos Aires, he authored a text entitled "Dialogues Between John Paul II and Fidel Castro".[^cite_note-Wilkinson,_Pope_will_visit_Cuba-392] John Paul was the first pope to visit Cuba. After a meeting between Francis and Cuban leader [Raúl Castro](221749.md) in May 2015, Castro said that he was considering returning to the Catholic Church.[^cite_note-Catholic_Cuba-393] He said in a televised news conference, "I read all the speeches of the pope, his commentaries, and if the pope continues this way, I will go back to praying and go back to the \[Catholic] church."[^cite_note-Yardley-2015-394]

As pope, Francis played a key role in the talks toward restoring [full diplomatic relations between the US and Cuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuban_Thaw), announced on 17 December 2014.[^cite_note-Bridge_to_Cuba-395] The Pope, along with the [Government of Canada](616255.md), was a behind-the-scenes broker of the agreement, taking the role following President Obama's request during his visit to the Pope in March 2014.[^cite_note-396] The success of the negotiations was credited to Francis because "as a religious leader with the confidence of both sides, he was able to convince the Obama and Castro administrations that the other side would live up to the deal".[^cite_note-Bridge_to_Cuba-395] En route to the United States for a [visit in September 2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_visit_by_Pope_Francis_to_North_America), the Pope stopped in Cuba.[^cite_note-Wilkinson,_Pope_will_visit_Cuba-392]

### Israeli–Palestinian conflict



Francis in [Bethlehem](4312.md) in May 2014

In May 2014, Francis visited [Israel](9282173.md) and the [Palestinian territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_territories).[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-Sales-398] Francis offered symbolic gestures to both sides in the [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md).[^cite_note-Sales-398] In addition to visiting the [Western Wall](75225.md), [Yad Vashem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yad_Vashem), and the [Church of the Holy Sepulchre](7810.md), he became the first pope to visit the [grave of Theodor Herzl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Herzl), entered the [West Bank](33209.md) from Jordan rather than Israel, and invited [Palestinian Authority](24093.md) president [Mahmoud Abbas](232595.md) and Israeli president [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres) to a prayer summit at the Vatican—both accepted.[^cite_note-Sales-398] He also visited [Bethlehem](4312.md), where he gave a speech alongside Abbas, and celebrated Mass at the [Church of the Nativity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_the_Nativity).[^cite_note-Sales-398] At the invitation of Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md), he visited the [Victims of Acts of Terror Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victims_of_Acts_of_Terror_Memorial); at the invitation of Palestinian authorities, he prayed at a portion of the [Israeli West Bank barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_West_Bank_barrier).[^cite_note-Sales-398] In addition to meetings with Peres and Netanyahu, Francis met [Grand Mufti of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Mufti_of_Jerusalem) [Muhammad Ahmad Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ahmad_Hussein), Chief Rabbis [Yitzhak Yosef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Yosef) and [David Lau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Lau), and [Rabbi of the Western Wall and the Holy Places](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabbi_of_the_Western_Wall_and_the_Holy_Places) [Shmuel Rabinowitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shmuel_Rabinowitz).[^cite_note-Sales-398]

In May 2015, Francis welcomed Abbas to the Vatican and said that: "The angel of peace destroys the evil spirit of war. I thought about you: may you be an angel of peace."[^cite_note-399] The Vatican signed a treaty [recognizing the state of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_the_State_of_Palestine).[^cite_note-400] The Vatican issued statements concerning the hope that peace talks could resume between Israel and Palestine. Abbas's visit was on the occasion of the canonization of two Palestinian nuns.[^cite_note-401]

On 13 May 2015, Vatican City announced the intention to sign its first treaty with the [State of Palestine](241405.md) after formally recognizing it as a state in February 2013.[^cite_note-402]

In May 2021, amid [clashes in Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Israel%E2%80%93Palestine_crisis), Francis reiterated calls for peace between Israel and Palestinians during his *_[Regina caeli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regina_caeli)_* address.[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404]

Francis condemned the [October 7 attacks](74998317.md) and also criticized Israel's actions in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) during the subsequent [Gaza war](74998519.md), saying that "terror should not justify terror" and describing Israel's airstrikes as "cruelty, this is not war."[^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406] He condemned [the killing of two Palestinian Christian women by an IDF sniper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Nahida_and_Samar_Anton) in Gaza, calling it "terrorism".[^cite_note-407] Throughout the war, Francis had called for an immediate ceasefire, the release of all [hostages](75025451.md),[^cite_note-408] and the implementation of a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution).[^cite_note-409] In November 2024, Pope Francis suggested that the international community should investigate if Israel's campaign in Gaza is a [genocide](75668427.md) of the Palestinian people.[^cite_note-410] From October 2023 until the day he died, he spoke with the [only Catholic church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Family_Church,_Gaza) in the Gaza Strip every night.[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412] In his last public appearance he again called for a ceasefire and condemned the "deplorable humanitarian situation" in Gaza.[^cite_note-AP_News-2025-413][^cite_note-414]

### Migrant and refugee issues



Francis with South Korean president [Park Geun-hye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_Geun-hye), 14 August 2014

Francis made the plight of refugees and migrants "a core component of his pastoral work" and had defended their rights in [dialogue both with Europe and with the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Francis#Refugees_and_migrants). In 2019, he placed a statue in [St. Peter's Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Peter's_Square) to bring attention to the Christian imperative involved in their situation (Hebrews 13\:2).[^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417] In line with this policy, Francis had criticized neo-nationalists and [populists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Populists) who reject the acceptance of refugees.[^cite_note-418][^cite_note-419]

In April 2016, Francis, along with [Ecumenical Patriarch Bartholomew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bartholomew_I_of_Constantinople) and [Archbishop Ieronimos II of Athens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ieronymos_II_of_Athens), visited the [Moria refugee camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moria_refugee_camp) on the Greek island of [Lesbos](2633994.md) in order to call the attention of the world to the [2015 European migrant crisis](46415102.md). There, the three Christian leaders signed a joint declaration.[^cite_note-420]

In February 2025, following the election to a second term of US president Donald Trump there were mass deportations and swingeing cuts to international aid by the new administration, defended by Vice President [JD Vance](53396477.md), a Catholic, by recasting the Catholic idea of *[ordo amoris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordo_amoris)* (the right ordering of one's love) as a justification for nativism. Francis wrote what had been described as "an extraordinary and excoriating response to US bishops". He cited the [parable of the Good Samaritan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parable_of_the_Good_Samaritan), described the *ordo amoris* as the love that "builds a fraternity open to all, without exception" and criticized the focus on solely family, community or national identity as "\[introducing] an ideological criterion that distorts social life and imposes the will of the strongest".[^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422]

### Sovereign Military Order of Malta



Francis with Polish prime minister [Mateusz Morawiecki](48505487.md) and his family, Vatican City, June 2018

In January 2017, Francis demanded the resignation of [Matthew Festing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Festing), the 79th [Prince and Grand Master](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Princes_and_Grand_Masters_of_the_Sovereign_Military_Order_of_Malta) of the [Sovereign Military Order of Malta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sovereign_Military_Order_of_Malta).[^cite_note-423] The Pope's demand was a response to Festing and Cardinal [Raymond Leo Burke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raymond_Leo_Burke) dismissing Baron [Albrecht von Boeselager](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albrecht_von_Boeselager) from his position in the Order of Malta. Fra' Giacomo Dalla Torre del Tempio di Sanguinetto was appointed leader in May 2017.[^cite_note-424]

### Russia and Ukraine



Francis and Brazilian president [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md), in 2023

Following the [February 2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md), a major escalation of the [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md), Francis visited the Russian embassy in Rome, an unprecedented action.[^cite_note-425] He called Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) to express "sorrow" as the Vatican worked to find "room for negotiation" to end the war.[^cite_note-426] The day after the invasion began in February 2022, Francis assured [Sviatoslav Shevchuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sviatoslav_Shevchuk), the [major archbishop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_archbishop) of the [Ukrainian Greek Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Greek_Catholic_Church), that "he would do everything he can to help end the Ukraine conflict".[^cite_note-427] During 27 February 2022 [Angelus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelus) address, Francis called for peace, saying, "Silence the weapons!"[^cite_note-428] At a September 2022 interfaith event in Kazakhstan, Francis urged [Patriarch Kirill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriarch_Kirill_of_Moscow) not to become an "altar boy" of Putin's.[^cite_note-Giuffrida-2022-429]

Throughout the war, Francis had called for an end to armed conflict.[^cite_note-Smith2023-430] Initially, he avoided specific criticism of Russia and President [Putin](32817.md), frustrating many Ukrainians.[^cite_note-Horowitz2024-431] Later, he described Ukraine as "martyred" and prayed for the victims of [Russian aggression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_wars_involving_Russia) but still did not directly criticize Putin or the Russian government.[^cite_note-Horowitz2024-431][^cite_note-Allen2023-432] His statements aligned more with countries like Brazil, India, and China[^cite_note-Allen2023-432] rather than the US and Europe—a stance some attribute to his distrust of America.[^cite_note-433] Francis warned against what he called a "simplistic good versus evil perception of the conflict", saying that a world leader who he did not name told him that NATO was "barking at the gates" of Russia, which led him to believe that the conflict was "somehow either provoked or not prevented."[^cite_note-Giuffrida-2022-429] These remarks damaged the Vatican's standing as a mediator in the conflict because supporters of Ukraine saw them as echoing Russian narratives about the war.[^cite_note-Mikhelidze2023-434]

Francis's blanket denunciations of arms transfers and the weapons industry[^cite_note-Horowitz2024-431][^cite_note-435] seemed to condemn [Western military aid to Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War).[^cite_note-Allen2023-432] In a September 2022 press conference, seven months into the war, Francis said that it was "licit" and justified for Ukraine [to defend itself](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-defence_in_international_law) but called for a negotiated settlement (saying that there must be "dialogue with any power that is at war, even if it is with the aggressor" and even when "it stinks").[^cite_note-White2022-436][^cite_note-437][^cite_note-Pullella-438] He also suggested that arms transfers to Ukraine were "a political decision which it can be moral, morally acceptable, if it is done under conditions of morality."[^cite_note-White2022-436] He later said that Ukrainians were a "noble" people and recounted Cardinal [Konrad Krajewski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konrad_Krajewski)'s reports of the "savage acts, the monstrosity, the tortured bodies" inflicted upon Ukraine.[^cite_note-Pullella-438]

Francis's stances were rooted in part in his hope that the Vatican could broker a peace deal between Ukraine and Russia, a possibility that analysts viewed as extremely unlikely.[^cite_note-Horowitz2024-431] He dispatched two high-ranking Vatican officials—Cardinals Krajewski and [Michael Czerny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Czerny)—as envoys on several trips to Ukraine in 2022.[^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441] which was considered a highly unusual move of Vatican diplomacy.[^cite_note-442] In March 2022, Francis [consecrated both Russia and Ukraine to the Immaculate Heart of Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consecration_of_Russia_to_the_Immaculate_Heart_of_Mary).[^cite_note-443] Francis said in April 2023, during a trip to [Budapest](36787.md), that he was working on a secret "mission" to bring peace and return [Ukrainian children abducted by Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_abductions_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War).[^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445] However, Francis's efforts to position the Vatican as a mediator were unsuccessful.[^cite_note-Allen2023-432][^cite_note-Mikhelidze2023-434]

In early October 2022, Francis for the first time directly appealed to Putin to halt the "spiral of violence and death" in Ukraine.[^cite_note-Pullella2022-446] In the same speech, Francis asked Ukrainian president Zelenskyy to be open about "serious peace proposals" while recognizing that Ukraine had suffered an "aggression" and saying that he was "pained about the suffering of the Ukrainian people".[^cite_note-PullellaOct2022-447]

### Armenia



Pope Francis and [Catholicos Garegin II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garegin_II) at an ecumenic ceremony in the [Republic Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_Square,_Yerevan), [Yerevan](34352.md), Armenia, June 2016

On the [100th anniversary of the Armenian genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/100th_anniversary_of_the_Armenian_genocide) in April 2015, Francis held a Vatican Mass where he explicitly described the World War I slaughter of Armenians as "the first genocide of the 20th century", inciting diplomatic anger from Turkey.[^cite_note-448]

In June 2016, Francis made a historic three-day visit to [Armenia](10918072.md) under his "Visit to the First Christian Nation" pilgrimage, where he honored the victims of the [Armenian genocide](140376.md) at the [Tsitsernakaberd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsitsernakaberd) memorial and joined prayers in the [Etchmiadzin Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etchmiadzin_Cathedral).[^cite_note-449] He again explicitly referred to the 1915 tragedy as "the first genocide of the 20th century".[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451] He signed a joint declaration with [Catholicos Garegin II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garegin_II) of the [Armenian Apostolic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Apostolic_Church), citing a strengthening of ties between Armenian Apostolic and Catholic churches.[^cite_note-452]

In October 2023, in the midst of the second Nagorno-Karabakh conflict, Francis called for peace in the region following the [displacement of over 100,000 ethnic Armenians](74922032.md) from Karabakh.[^cite_note-453] The following year, during a meeting with the Armenian bishops in the [Vatican](32408.md), Francis again held prayers for Armenia: "... how can we not turn our thoughts to Armenia (...) in our prayers, particularly for all those fleeing Nagorno-Karabakh and for the many displaced families seeking refuge?"[^cite_note-454]

### In the Muslim world



[Istiqlal Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istiqlal_Mosque,_Jakarta) Grand Imam [Nasaruddin Umar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasaruddin_Umar) kisses the forehead of Francis, September 2024

Francis condemned the [persecution of Christians by ISIL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Christians_by_ISIL) and supported the use of force to stop Islamic militants from attacking religious minorities in Iraq.[^cite_note-455] In January 2018, Francis met [Yazidi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yazidi_people) refugees in Europe, expressed his support for their right to religious freedom, and called upon the international community "not to remain a silent and unresponsive spectator" to the [Yazidi genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yazidi_genocide).[^cite_note-456]

In February 2019, Francis visited [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md), United Arab Emirates, on the invitation of [Mohammed bin Zayed Al Nahyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_bin_Zayed_Al_Nahyan). Francis became the first pope to celebrate Mass on the [Arabian Peninsula](47858.md), attended by more than 120,000 attendees at the [Zayed Sports City Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zayed_Sports_City_Stadium).[^cite_note-457]

In March 2021, Francis held a [historic meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis's_visit_to_Iraq) with Iraq's top [Shi'ite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shi'ite) cleric, [Grand Ayatollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Ayatollah) [Ali al-Sistani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_al-Sistani), and visited [Ur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ur_of_the_Chaldees), a site traditionally identified as the birthplace of the prophet [Abraham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham). He and the Iraqi cleric urged the Muslim and Christian communities to work together for peaceful coexistence.[^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459]

In September 2024, Francis [visited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis'_visit_to_Indonesia) [Indonesia](14579.md), the country with the largest Muslim population,[^cite_note-460] where he attended inter-religious dialogue in [Istiqlal Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istiqlal_Mosque,_Jakarta) in Jakarta and was welcomed by the Grand Imam [Nasaruddin Umar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasaruddin_Umar).[^cite_note-Vatican_News-2024-461] Francis and the Grand Imam additionally signed the Joint Declaration of Istiqlal 2024, underscoring that the values common to all religious traditions be effectively promoted to "defeat the culture of violence and indifference" and promote reconciliation and peace.[^cite_note-Vatican_News-2024-461] The declaration was also read and attended by representatives from other religions, including [Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism), [Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindus), [Buddhists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhists), [Confucians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucians), and [Folk religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_religion).[^cite_note-462]

### G7

Further information: [50th G7 summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/50th_G7_summit)

Pope Francis was the first pontiff to participate in the [G7](1196634.md), a meeting of leaders of the largest developed economies in the world. During his speech at the G7 forum in Italy, he stressed that humanity is in great danger due to the wars that are taking place such as the wars [in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) and [in Gaza](74998519.md). He also stated that the excessive use of [artificial intelligence](1164.md) is [posing a risk to jobs](64681740.md), and remarked on reproductive practices without specifically mentioning [abortion](765.md).[^cite_note-463][^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465][^cite_note-466]

### Elsewhere



Crowd at the [Koševo City Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ko%C5%A1evo_City_Stadium) in [Sarajevo](26786.md), celebrating a Mass with Francis, June 2015

In September 2015, Francis visited the [United Nations Headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Headquarters) in New York City where he addressed the [UN General Assembly](31957.md); following his speech, he visited the [National September 11 Memorial and Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_September_11_Memorial_and_Museum).[^cite_note-Times_2015-467] In June 2023, Francis issued an address to the [UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council) while recovering from abdominal surgery; the statement was read by Vatican official [Paul Gallagher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Gallagher_\(bishop\)) on the Pope's behalf.[^cite_note-468][^cite_note-469]

After the [2017 Catalan independence referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Catalan_independence_referendum) that originated the [2017–18 Spanish constitutional crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017%E2%80%932018_Spanish_constitutional_crisis) on 1 October 2017, Francis communicated to the Spanish ambassador to the Holy See Gerardo Bugallo that the Vatican would not recognize secessionist or self-determination movements that were not the result of decolonization.[^cite_note-470]

## Public image

See also: [List of places and things named after Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_places_and_things_named_after_Pope_Francis)

| External videos |
| --- |
|  |
| video icon [Why the only future worth building includes everyone](https://www.ted.com/talks/his_holiness_pope_francis_why_the_only_future_worth_building_includes_everyone?language=en), [TED talks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TED_\(conference\)), April 2017, 17\:51, in Italian with subtitles in 22 languages |

Francis was frequently described as a [progressive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressivism).[^cite_note-471] Commentator [William Saletan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Saletan) described Francis as liberal and fundamentally anti-conservative in his analysis of Francis's first long interview after becoming pope, highlighting Francis's statements "God is to be encountered in the world of today" and "God manifests himself in historical revelation, in history".[^cite_note-472] Other have contested descriptions of Francis as liberal.[^cite_note-473] In 2014, the Vatican criticized some Italian news agencies, as well as the [Agence France-Presse](471981.md), for reporting that a comment Francis made was suggestive of an opening toward acceptance of same-sex marriage or civil unions; a Vatican spokesperson said the pope's remark was taken out of context.[^cite_note-474]

Important aspects of Francis's public image include "his recognizable humanity" and gestures of humility, as well as his efforts to preserve his autonomy amid [Roman Curia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Curia) bureaucracy.[^cite_note-Achanging-475] He was a frequent user of [landline telephones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landline_telephone); he reportedly had never owned a computer or [mobile phone](19644137.md).[^cite_note-Achanging-475] Shortly before his death, Francis donated most of his personal wealth, approximately €200,000, to support a pasta-making project at a youth prison in Rome.[^cite_note-476]

In December 2013, both *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Magazine)* and *[The Advocate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Advocate_\(LGBT_magazine\))* magazines named Francis as their "Person of the Year"; *Esquire* magazine named him as the "Best-dressed man" for 2013, citing his simpler vestments.[^cite_note-477] *[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone)* magazine followed in January 2014 by making him their featured front cover.[^cite_note-478][^cite_note-479] *[Fortune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_\(magazine\))* magazine also ranked Francis as number one in their list of 50 greatest leaders.[^cite_note-480] He was included in *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* lists of [most powerful people in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_list_of_The_World's_Most_Powerful_People) in 2014[^cite_note-481] and 2016.[^cite_note-482]

In March 2013, a new song was dedicated to Francis and released in Brazilian Portuguese, European Portuguese, and Italian, titled *_Come Puoi_* (*How You Can*).[^cite_note-nofrills-133] A street in [La Plata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Plata), Argentina, was renamed Papa Francisco in his honor.[^cite_note-street-483] The [Argentine Chamber of Deputies](751124.md) passed legislation to mint a [commemorative coin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commemorative_coin) as a tribute to Francis in 2013.[^cite_note-484][^cite_note-485] As of 2013, sales of papal souvenirs, a sign of popularity, were up.[^cite_note-NYT52513-486]

Francis presided over his first joint public wedding ceremony in a Nuptial Mass for 20 couples from the Archdiocese of Rome on 14 September 2014, a few weeks before the start of 5–19 October [Third Extraordinary General Assembly of the Synod of Bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Extraordinary_General_Assembly_of_the_Synod_of_Bishops) (the Synod on the Family).[^cite_note-487][^cite_note-489]

In 2016, Francis became the first pope to create an [Instagram](31591547.md) account.[^cite_note-490] He broke records after having gained over one million followers in under 12 hours of the account being up.[^cite_note-491] On 26 November 2020, Francis became the first pope to write an op-ed for *The New York Times*, addressing issues such as COVID-19 restrictions on public gatherings and the need for global solidarity.[^cite_note-492][^cite_note-493]

Francis was a longtime supporter of the football club [San Lorenzo de Almagro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Lorenzo_de_Almagro), where his father played in [the basketball department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Lorenzo_de_Almagro_\(basketball\)). The Argentine club proceeded to visit Francis at the Vatican after winning both the [2013 Torneo Inicial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013%E2%80%9314_Argentine_Primera_Divisi%C3%B3n_season) and the [2014 Copa Libertadores](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Copa_Libertadores), where he was gifted replicas of both trophies and a glove of goalkeeper [Sebastian Torrico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Torrico).[^cite_note-494][^cite_note-495]

## Health



Francis with then-President of Indonesia [Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo) in September 2024. Francis had publicly used a wheelchair since 2022.

Elected at the age of 76, Francis was reported to be healthy; his doctors had said the lung tissue removed in his youth did not significantly affect his health.[^cite_note-The_Guardian-2013b-496] The only concern would be decreased respiratory reserve if he had a respiratory infection.[^cite_note-Park-2013-497] The Pope had suffered from chronic lung damage, due in part to the lung excision he had as a young man. In the last few years of his life, he was prone to bouts of [influenza](19572217.md) and [bronchitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronchitis) in the winter. Knee problems and [sciatica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sciatica) prompted him to frequently use a wheelchair, walker, or cane.[^cite_note-498] In 2021, the Pope's health problems prompted rumors that he might resign,[^cite_note-499] which Francis dismissed.[^cite_note-500] In June 2022, after undergoing treatment to his knee, Francis canceled planned trips to the [Democratic Republic of the Congo](76762.md) and [South Sudan](32350676.md).[^cite_note-501]

In March 2023, Francis was hospitalized in Rome with a respiratory infection.[^cite_note-502] He returned to celebrate the [Easter Vigil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_Vigil) Mass on [Holy Saturday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Saturday), in April.[^cite_note-503] In June, Francis underwent abdominal surgery after suffering from a [hernia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hernia).[^cite_note-504] He acknowledged that his recurring mobility problems had precipitated the beginning of what [Reuters](18998750.md) termed "a new, slower phase of his papacy",[^cite_note-505] although he was praised by disabled Catholics for making his "disability part of his visible identity".[^cite_note-506]

In February 2025, Francis entered [Gemelli Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gemelli_Hospital) in Rome due to [bronchitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronchitis).[^cite_note-507] He remained for more than a month after developing a polymicrobial infection of his respiratory tract and bilateral pneumonia.[^cite_note-508][^cite_note-509][^cite_note-Povoledo-2025-510] *[Vatican News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vatican_News)* described his condition as [critical](30864674.md) and reported that he was given [blood transfusions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_transfusions) and [high-flow oxygen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heated_humidified_high-flow_therapy).[^cite_note-511][^cite_note-512] Eventually, Francis was put on [mechanical ventilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mechanical_ventilation) for a number of days, and suffered two episodes of "[acute respiratory insufficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respiratory_failure)".[^cite_note-513] After the infection improved, he was discharged from the hospital on 23 March,[^cite_note-514] immediately after blessing a crowd from his balcony. He was expected to spend at least two months recuperating at his home in [Domus Sanctae Marthae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domus_Sanctae_Marthae) in Vatican City,[^cite_note-515] maintaining a reduced work schedule.[^cite_note-516][^cite_note-517] He appeared in public for the first time since his hospitalization on 6 April.[^cite_note-Povoledo-2025-510]

## Death

Main article: [Death and funeral of Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_and_funeral_of_Pope_Francis)

See also: [Reactions to the death of Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactions_to_the_death_of_Pope_Francis)



Procession of the body of Pope Francis in the Vatican



Francis's tomb in [Santa Maria Maggiore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Maria_Maggiore)

Francis's last public appearance was at St. Peter's Square in Vatican City on [Easter Sunday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_Sunday), 20 April 2025, where he gave his final Easter address and called for a ceasefire in [Gaza](74998519.md).[^cite_note-LastAppearance-18][^cite_note-518][^cite_note-AP_News-2025-413] He died at 07\:35 [local time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_European_Summer_Time) (UTC+02\:00) on [Easter Monday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_Monday), 21 April 2025, aged 88, in his residence in [Domus Sanctae Marthae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domus_Sanctae_Marthae).[^cite_note-519][^cite_note-APDeath-520] His death, announced by Cardinal [Kevin Farrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Farrell) on the [Vatican's TV channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vatican_News) and in a video statement,[^cite_note-521] was caused by a [cerebral stroke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cerebral_stroke), which led to a [coma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coma) and irreversible [cardiac arrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiac_arrest).[^cite_note-522][^cite_note-523]

The pope's death began a [papal interregnum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_interregnum) and a nine-day period of mourning known as the *novendiales* ([Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_language) for 'nine days'). His funeral took place on 26 April 2025.[^cite_note-524] Cardinal electors arrived in Rome to attend the congregation of [cardinals](6221.md) and decided that 7 May 2025 would be the start of the [conclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_conclave) set to elect Francis's successor.[^cite_note-525] On 8 May, Robert Francis Prevost, who was made a cardinal by Francis in 2023, was elected as [Pope Leo XIV](54520372.md).

Francis's spiritual testament, dated 29 June 2022, repeated his wish to be buried at the [Basilica of Santa Maria Maggiore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basilica_of_Santa_Maria_Maggiore) in Rome. Upon his death, he was laid to rest there in accordance with his testament, becoming the first pope to be interred in the Santa Maria Maggiore since [Clement IX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clement_IX) in 1669.[^cite_note-526][^cite_note-Vatican_News-2025-527] His testament ended:

> May the Lord grant a fitting reward to all those who have loved me and who continue to pray for me. The suffering that has marked the final part of my life, I offer to the Lord, for peace in the world and for fraternity among peoples.[^cite_note-Vatican_News-2025-527]

## Legacy



Pope Francis cameo ivory on wood carving at [Notre Dame de Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Notre_Dame_de_Paris), France

Francis's papacy coincided with a period of widespread change and reckoning within the global Catholic order and within society at large. Throughout his papacy, he was noted for his support for the plight of [refugees](45547.md), [migrants](4599312.md), and the [impoverished](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty).[^cite_note-528] Since their beginnings, he had been outspoken in his criticism of the wars in [Ukraine](42085878.md), [Sudan](73556699.md), [Gaza](74998519.md), [DR Congo](14687290.md), and [Myanmar](69807451.md), notwithstanding numerous other conflicts and acts of suppression against the civilian population globally.[^cite_note-Munoko-2025-529][^cite_note-530][^cite_note-531] He appointed more than 80% of the [cardinals](6221.md) that [elected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_conclave) his [successor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_succession), [Leo XIV](54520372.md), notably reducing the European share of the [College of Cardinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Cardinals) from a majority to a plurality.[^cite_note-Pullella-2025-532][^cite_note-533] He was celebrated for his outreach to [China](1714754.md) and the [African continent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Africa), his tolerance towards differing faith communities, and his attention to younger Catholic adherents and the changing nature of the faith.[^cite_note-Munoko-2025-529][^cite_note-534] Additionally, he formalized the church's policy of opposition to the [death penalty](5902.md) in all cases.[^cite_note-535][^cite_note-536][^cite_note-Pullella-2025-532]

Under his papacy, he maintained the prohibition of [women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_the_Catholic_Church) being ordained clergy, or being appointed to the [College of Bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Bishops) and the [Cardinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Cardinals). However, Francis made significant strides towards increasing women's presence in the senior and central administration of the church.[^cite_note-McElwee-2025-537][^cite_note-Pullella-2025-532][^cite_note-Horowitz-2025-538] He was the first to grant them voting rights within the [Synod of Bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synod_of_Bishops_in_the_Catholic_Church), and increased their presence in functions and institutions of the Church that had previously been restricted to or dominated by men.[^cite_note-NBC_News-2021-257][^cite_note-539] He had nonetheless been criticized by some as having only produced reform within existing frameworks of gender division within the Catholic Church, doing little to advance serious, radical reform of its institutions to ensure an ideal of inclusion and parity.[^cite_note-McElwee-2025-537]

He set himself apart from other Popes in [upholding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditionis_custodes) the Church's departure from the [Tridentine Mass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tridentine_Mass), which had only been [loosely enforced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missale_Romanum_\(apostolic_constitution\)) by both predecessors whose reign occurred after the institution of the [reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacrosanctum_Concilium) of the [Second Vatican Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council).[^cite_note-Horowitz-2021-382][^cite_note-540][^cite_note-Pullella-2025-532]\[^cite_note-[www.vatican.va-541](http://www.vatican.va-541/)] Though it was not banned outright during his tenure, he nonetheless greatly increased Vatican oversight over the facilitation of the ritual, and restricted the right of new priests to engage in the practice. These policies were controversial among [traditionalist Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditionalist_Catholicism).\[^cite_note-[www.vatican.va-541](http://www.vatican.va-541/)][^cite_note-542]

In 2022, he issued the first apology by the Vatican for its role in the [cultural erasure and forced assimilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Indian_residential_school_system) of [First Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Nations_in_Canada) peoples in [Canada](5042916.md) from the late 19th to mid-20th centuries.[^cite_note-543] His endorsement of the [blessing of individuals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiducia_supplicans) in [same-sex relationships](19771642.md) earned him praise from many progressive outlets within and outside the Church and criticism from conservative circles. Some groups, though, have been critical of the extent of his [tolerance on various LGBTQ issues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis_and_LGBTQ_topics), particularly the question of his acceptance of [transgender identity](19904525.md) and his answer to the larger issue of [homosexuality](10380708.md), [queer identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queer), and [sin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_views_on_sin).[^cite_note-544][^cite_note-545][^cite_note-Horowitz-2025-538] The progressive stances he held drew significant criticism from conservative elements within the college.[^cite_note-Pullella-2025-532]

## Distinctions

### Titles and styles

The official form of address of the Pope in English was *His Holiness Pope Francis*. *Holy Father* was among the other [honorifics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/His_holiness#Catholic_church) used for popes.[^cite_note-546]

  [^cite_note-563]

  [^cite_note-564]

- The composer [Ludger Stühlmeyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludger_St%C3%BChlmeyer) dedicated his work *Klangrede – Sonnengesang des [Franziskus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_of_Assisi)*, for choir (SATB) and instruments – to Pope Francis (*Suae Sanctitati Papae Francisci dedicat*). First performance: Capella Mariana 4 October 2015.

  [^cite_note-565]

- The rodent species *[Oecomys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oecomys) franciscorum*, discovered in 2016, was named after both Francis and [Francisco Maldonado da Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Maldonado_da_Silva), a victim of the [Peruvian Inquisition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peruvian_Inquisition).

  [^cite_note-566]

- In the [oratorio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oratorio) *[Laudato si'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laudato_si'_\(oratorio\))* by [Peter Reulein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Reulein) (music) written on a [libretto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libretto) by [Helmut Schlegel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helmut_Schlegel) [OFM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Friars_Minor), the figure of Francis appears. It was first performed at [Limburg Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limburg_Cathedral) in 2016.

  [^cite_note-Laudato_si-score-567]

  [^cite_note-Laudato_si-Liebfrauen-568]

## Coat of arms

Main article: [Coat of arms of Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Pope_Francis)

Further information: [Papal coats of arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_coats_of_arms)

|  | NotesPope Francis's initial [grant of arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grant_of_arms) by the [Holy See](13393.md) was as [Archbishop of Buenos Aires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Buenos_Aires), when he was Cardinal Bergoglio, in which the depiction of the star and spikenard were [tinctured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tincture_\(heraldry\)) [argent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argent). Following his election as [pope](23056.md), these are now tinctured [or](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Or_\(heraldry\)). The first version of Francis's coat of arms released by the [Vatican Press Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vatican_Press_Office) depicted a five-pointed star from Bergoglio's archiepiscopal version, but his coat of arms upon election as pope assumed an eight-pointed star, with the depiction of the spikenard also being suitably differenced.[^cite_note-NCR-128] Adopted18 March 2013 Helm [Bishop's miter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitre) Escutcheon*Azure on a sun in splendour or the [IHS Christogram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Name_of_Jesus) ensigned with a [cross pattée](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross_patt%C3%A9e) fiché piercing the [H](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Name_of_Jesus) gules all above three nails fanwise points to center sable, and in dexter base a [mullet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_\(heraldry\)) of eight points and in sinister base a [spikenard flower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lavandula) or.*[^cite_note-569] Mott&#x6F;***MISERANDO ATQUE ELIGENDO*** ([Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_language) for 'By giving mercy and by choosing') Other elements[Keys of Peter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keys_of_Peter) behind the [shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Escutcheon_\(heraldry\)) and papal [mantling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mantling) Symbolism*Jesuit emblem*: Emblem of the [Society of Jesus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Society_of_Jesus), in reference to Francis being a Jesuit. This [charge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charge_\(heraldry\)) displays a radiating sun within which is the [monogram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monogram) of the [Holy Name of Jesus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Name_of_Jesus) in red, with a red cross surmounting the [H](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christogram) and three black nails below the H.[^cite_note-OR-127] *Eight-pointed star*: A long-standing symbol of the [Virgin Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virgin_Mary). *Spikenard*: The spikenard flower represents [Saint Joseph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Joseph); in [Hispanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic) iconographic tradition Saint Joseph is often depicted holding a branch of spikenard.[^cite_note-VIS-570] |
| --- | --- |

## Media

By 2015, there were two biographical films about Francis: *[Call Me Francesco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Call_Me_Francesco)* (Italy, 2015), starring [Rodrigo de la Serna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodrigo_de_la_Serna), and *[Francis: Pray for me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis:_Pray_for_me)* (Argentina, 2015), starring [Darío Grandinetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dar%C3%ADo_Grandinetti).[^cite_note-571] A music album, *[Wake Up!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wake_Up!_\(Pope_Francis_album\))*, consisting of speeches by Francis accompanied by music was released on 27 November 2015.[^cite_note-572][^cite_note-573][^cite_note-574]

Several documentaries have been created about Francis. *[Pope Francis: A Man of His Word](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis:_A_Man_of_His_Word)* (2018) is a documentary film co-written and directed by [Wim Wenders](32902.md).[^cite_note-575][^cite_note-576] On 21 October 2020, the documentary *[Francesco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_\(2020_film\))* directed by film producer [Evgeny Afineevsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evgeny_Afineevsky) premiered.[^cite_note-Vatican_News-2020-577][^cite_note-Winfield-2020-578] On 4 October 2022, the documentary *[The Letter: A Message for our Earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Letter:_A_Message_for_our_Earth)* premiered on YouTube Originals, directed by Nicolas Brown and produced by Off The Fence in partnership with [Laudato Si' Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laudato_Si'_Movement).[^cite_note-579]

Francis was played by [Jonathan Pryce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Pryce) in the [biographical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biographical) film *[The Two Popes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Two_Popes)* (2019).[^cite_note-580]
